. For an irrational
r
, (
3.9a
,
3.9b
) encode the more complex,
quasiperiodic
, behavior of
A^{\delta}
along
\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
.

Remark 3.2
.

(a).

One can interpret
\{{\bm{x}}\mapsto\!\mskip 1.5muA^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s),s\in\mathbb{R}\}
as a family of two-dimensional dislocations of
A^{\delta}
in the
{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}
–direction. In fact, for
s\in\mathbb{R}
fixed, assuming for simplicity that
\kappa(\zeta)={\rm sgn}(\zeta)
,
{\bm{x}}\mapsto A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)
models a sharp junction, in the direction transverse to the interface
\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}-s\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}
, between media modeled by
{\bm{x}}\mapsto{\operatorname{Id}}+\delta\,a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}
and
{\bm{x}}\mapsto{\operatorname{Id}}-\delta\,a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}
. Dislocations may be studied independently from one another if
r
is rational, but we see from (
3.9a
,
3.9b
) that these dislocations are all coupled if
r
is irrational.

(b).

The existence of a function
A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}
with the translation-invariance properties (
3.9a
,
3.9b
) and such that
A^{\delta}({\bm{x}})=A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},0)
is closely related to the quasiperiodicity of
A^{\delta}
along
\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
. More precisely, a function
q\in\mathscr{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})
is said to be
quasiperiodic
of order
n\geq 1
if there exist a
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
–periodic function
Q\in\mathscr{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and a vector
\bm{\theta}=(\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
\theta_{j}\neq 0
, such that

\forall\;y\in\mathbb{R},\quad q(y)=Q(y\,\bm{\theta}):=Q(y\mskip 1.5mu\theta_{1},\dots,y\mskip 1.5mu\theta_{n}).

(3.10)

Examples of quasiperiodic functions include finite sums and products of periodic functions. For a detailed study of quasiperiodic (and more generally of almost periodic) functions, we refer to the monographs
[
19
,
21
,
112
,
67
]
; see also
[
117
]
.

We claim that for a fixed
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
, the function
q_{\zeta}:y\in\mathbb{R}\mapsto A^{\delta}(y\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\zeta\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{2})
, which represents
A^{\delta}
in the
{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
–direction, is quasiperiodic of order
2
in the sense of (
3.10
). In fact, from
{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}={\mathbf{v}}_{1}+r{\mathbf{v}}_{2}
and
{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}={\mathbf{v}}_{2}
, it follows that

q_{\zeta}(y)={\operatorname{Id}}-\delta\,\kappa(\delta\mskip 1.5mu\zeta)\,a\big(y\,{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+(y\mskip 1.5mur+\zeta)\,{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\big)\,\sigma_{2},\quad\forall\;y\in\mathbb{R}.

In particular, with
\bm{\theta}:=(1,r)
, we have
q_{\zeta}(y)=Q_{\zeta}(y\,\bm{\theta})=Q_{\zeta}(y,y\mskip 1.5mur)
, where

Q_{\zeta}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2}):={\operatorname{Id}}-\delta\,\kappa(\delta\mskip 1.5mu\zeta)\,a\big(\tau_{1}\,{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+(\tau_{2}+\zeta)\,{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\big)\,\sigma_{2},\quad\forall\;(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

Since
a(\cdot)
is
\Lambda
–periodic, it follows that
Q_{\zeta}
is
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
–periodic. Hence,
q_{\zeta}
is quasiperiodic in the sense of (
3.10
). The link between the augmented functions
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\zeta)\mapsto Q_{\zeta}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
and
({\bm{x}},s)\mapsto A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)
follows from the change of variables

(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\zeta)\mapsto({\bm{x}},s):=(\tau_{1}\,{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+(\tau_{2}+\zeta)\,{\mathbf{v}}_{2},\,r\mskip 1.5mu\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}).

3.4.2.
Formulation of the eigenvalue problem for irrational edges

Edge states are nontrivial solutions of the eigenvalue equation
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{dw}}\,\Psi=E\,\Psi
that are oscillatory (plane wave-like) along the edge
\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
and decaying in the transverse direction. We now specify the behavior along
\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
of edge states in the irrational case, by exploiting the quasiperiodic nature of
(A^{\delta},V)
. The relations
A^{\delta}({\bm{x}})=A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},0)
and
V({\bm{x}})=V_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},0)
suggest looking for edge states in the form

\displaystyle\Psi({\bm{x}})=\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)\big|_{s=0},\quad\text{for}\quad{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

(3.11)

where
\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)
satisfies the degenerate elliptic equation

\big(-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}+V_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)=E\,\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s),\quad({\bm{x}},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}.

Moreover, we require decay in any direction transverse to the interface
\Gamma
defined by (
3.7
). For instance, choosing the vector
{\mathbf{a}}_{\perp}
defined by (
3.8
), we may impose

\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)\to 0,\quad|s|\to+\infty.

Finally, associated with the periodicity properties (
3.9a
,
3.9b
) of
(A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}},V_{\textnormal{aug}})
along the two-dimensional plane
\Gamma
, we introduce, for parallel quasi-momentum pairs,
(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},k_{0})\in[-\pi,\pi]^{2}
the pseudo-periodic boundary conditions within
\Gamma
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{r@{\ =\ }l@{\quad}l}\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{1},s+r)&\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5muk_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}\,\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s),&({\bm{x}},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R},\\[8.0pt]
\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{2},s-1)&\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5muk_{0}}\,\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s),&({\bm{x}},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R},\end{array}

which describe the oscillatory, or more precisely, the quasiperiodic behavior of
\Psi
along
\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
.

Note that for
E\in\mathbb{R}
and for a fixed pair
(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},k_{0})\in[-\pi,\pi]^{2}
, the above equations are satisfied by
\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}
if and only if
{({\bm{x}},s)\mapsto\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5muk_{0}\mskip 1.5mus}\,\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)}
satisfies the same equations for the quasi-momenta
(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}+r\mskip 1.5muk_{0},0)
. Therefore, we may take
k_{0}=0
with no loss of generality.

This leads to the
augmented edge state eigenvalue problem with parallel quasi-momentum
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\in[-\pi,\pi]
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ }l}\displaystyle\big(-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}+V_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)\mskip 1.5mu\big)&\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)=E\,\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s),\\[8.0pt]
\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{1},s+r)&=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5muk_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}\,\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s),\\[8.0pt]
\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{2},s-1)&=\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s),\\[8.0pt]
\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)\to 0,&|s|\to+\infty.\end{array}\right.

(3.12)

If an augmented edge state,
\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)
, exists and is continuous at
s=0
, then its restriction
\Psi({\bm{x}}):=\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},0)
is formally an edge state. Indeed,
\Psi
satisfies
(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{dw}}}-E)\,\Psi=0
, is quasiperiodic along the irrational line
\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
, and decays in the transverse (
{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}={\mathbf{v}}_{2}
) direction. The transverse decay property can be formally justified as follows: The second of the boundary conditions in (
3.12
) implies
\Psi({\bm{x}}+n\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{2})=\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}}+n\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{v}}_{2},0)=\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},n)
. Further, the third boundary condition in (
3.12
) implies: for any
\mathrm{y}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\lim_{|\zeta|\to+\infty}\Psi(\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\zeta\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{2})=\lim_{n\in\mathbb{Z},|n|\to+\infty}\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}(\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+n\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{2},0)=\lim_{n\in\mathbb{Z},|n|\to+\infty}\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}(\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},n)=0\ .

Remark 3.3
.

An important feature of the augmented eigenvalue problem (
3.12
) is that the principal part of the operator
-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}+V_{\textnormal{aug}}
is degenerate elliptic; it does not involve any derivative with respect to
s
, in contrast to a classical Schrödinger operator, such as:
-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}-\partial^{2}_{s}+V_{\textnormal{aug}}
.

4.
Multiscale expansion and effective Dirac operators

In this section, we extend the procedure in
[
61
,
64
,
111
,
43
]
and present a formal multiscale expansion of solutions of the augmented eigenvalue problem (
3.12
). The approximate edge states emerging from our asymptotic analysis suggest that, for irrational edges, there are infinitely many edge state curves bifurcating from the Dirac point for
0<\delta\ll 1
. In contrast to earlier studies of edge states for rational edges, we find that these bifurcations are controlled by an
infinite family
of Dirac effective operators. The multiple scale expansion is summarized in Section
4.4
.

4.1.
The ansatz

We are interested in constructing approximate solutions
\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}
to the augmented eigenvalue problem (
3.12
) which are of wave packet type, and in a sense, spectrally concentrated near
{\mathbf{K}}
. Thus, we take

k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu=({\mathbf{K}}+\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\quad\text{with}\quad(\mu,\delta)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+},\quad 0<\delta\ll 1.

The multiple scale structure of of
A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}
(see (
3.6
)) suggests that we seek
\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}
as a function of decoupled variables: a pair of
fast
variables
({\bm{x}},s)
associated to the augmented bulk honeycomb medium, and a
slow
variable
\zeta=\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})
associated with the slow variation of the domain wall. Thus we write

\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)=\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s,\zeta),\quad\zeta=\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}).

Substituting this ansatz into the eigenvalue problem (
3.12
), and applying the derivation rule

\nabla_{\bm{x}}\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)=\big(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\,D_{\zeta}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s,\zeta)\Big|_{\zeta=\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})},\quad\text{where}\quad\ D_{\zeta}:=-\mathrm{i}\,\partial_{\zeta}\ ,

we find that
\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}:({\bm{x}},s,\zeta)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{C}
satisfies an eigenvalue problem in the extended set of variables:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Big[\!-\big(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\mskip 1.5muD_{\zeta}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\cdot\big(\mskip 1.5mu{\operatorname{Id}}-\delta\mskip 1.5mu\kappa(\zeta)\mskip 1.5mua({\bm{x}})\mskip 1.5mu\sigma_{2}\mskip 1.5mu\big)

\displaystyle\big(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\mskip 1.5muD_{\zeta}\mskip 1.5mu\big)+V({\bm{x}})-E\Big]\,\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}=0,

(4.1a)

\displaystyle\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{1},s+r,\zeta)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}\,\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s,\zeta),

(4.1b)

\displaystyle\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{2},s-1,\zeta)

\displaystyle=\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s,\zeta),

(4.1c)

\displaystyle\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s,\zeta)

\displaystyle\to 0,\quad|\zeta|\to+\infty.

(4.1d)

If
\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s,\zeta)
is a solution of (
4.1
), then
\smash{\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s,\zeta)\big|_{\zeta=\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}}
is a solution of the eigenvalue problem eigenvalue problem (
3.12
).

Let
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
. Since our goal is to construct
\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}
perturbatively around the Dirac point
({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},E_{D})
, we look for a solution of the form

\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s,\zeta)=\exp\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\big(\mskip 1.5mu\delta\,(\mu+\widetilde{\gamma})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}+({\lambda}+{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\mskip 1.5mus\mskip 1.5mu\big)\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\,\psi({\bm{x}},\zeta),

(4.2)

where

{\bm{x}}\mapsto\psi({\bm{x}},\zeta)\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}},\quad\zeta\mapsto\psi({\bm{x}},\zeta)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}),\quad|\widetilde{\gamma}|\leq 1,\quad{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}.

(4.3)

Remark 4.1

(On the Ansatz (
4.2
)
for
\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s,\zeta)
)
.

Note that
{\bm{x}}\mapsto\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s,\zeta)
is formally a wave packet of bandwidth
\delta
; it is spectrally localized to a
\mathcal{O}(\delta)
–neighborhood of the Dirac point (
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star},E_{D})
. Here, the exponential dependence in
s
is motivated by the fact that the eigenvalue problem (
4.1a
) is invariant under translations in the
s
variable. Finally the parameters
\tilde{\gamma}
and
\lambda
will be chosen so that the conditions (
4.1b
)–(
4.1c
) and
(
4.3
) are consistent. Just below, we’ll impose this consistency and find that
\delta\tilde{\gamma}
and
\lambda
are “quantized”; see the relations (
4.6
) below.
We’ll then, in Section
4.2
, formulate the PDE problem for
\psi({\bm{x}},\zeta)
and, for admissible
\tilde{\gamma}
and
\lambda
, derive an asymptotic expansion of its solution in powers of
\delta
.

Let us now determine the pairs
(\widetilde{\gamma},{\lambda})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
for which the pseudo-periodic boundary conditions in (
4.1
) and (
4.3
) satisfied by
\psi
and
\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}
are compatible. Combining (
4.1b
) with the relation
\psi({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{1},\zeta)=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{1}}\,\psi({\bm{x}},\zeta)
, and (
4.1c
) with the relation
\psi({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{2},\zeta)=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,\psi({\bm{x}},\zeta)
, we obtain:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\quad}l}{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}=\delta\,\widetilde{\gamma}+({\lambda}+{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\mskip 1.5mur+{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+2\pi n&n\in\mathbb{Z},\\[4.0pt]
0=-{\lambda}-{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2}+{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2}+2\pi m&m\in\mathbb{Z}.\end{array}\right.

(4.4)

Here, we have also used that
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{1}={\mathbf{k}}_{1}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{1}=1
and
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2}={\mathbf{k}}_{1}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2}=0
. The second equation in (
4.4
) determines
{\lambda}
. Substituting this expression into the first equation, and using the relation
{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+r{\mathbf{v}}_{2}={\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
, we get

\delta\,\widetilde{\gamma}=({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}-2\pi(n+mr),\quad(m,n)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}.

(4.5)

We seek integers
m,n
and
\delta\,\widetilde{\gamma}\in[-\pi,\pi)
satisfying (
4.5
). The latter constraint implies

\displaystyle\delta\,\widetilde{\gamma}\ +\pi=({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}-2\pi(n+mr)+\pi

\displaystyle\in[0,2\pi)

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\,\widetilde{\gamma}}{2\pi}\ +\frac{1}{2}=\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}{2\pi}-(n+mr)+\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\in[0,1)

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\,\widetilde{\gamma}}{2\pi}\ +\frac{1}{2}=\operatorname{frac}\left(\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}{2\pi}-mr+\frac{1}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\in[0,1)

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\delta\,\widetilde{\gamma}=2\pi\ \operatorname{frac}\left(\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}{2\pi}-mr+\frac{1}{2}\right)-\pi

\displaystyle\in[-\pi,\pi),

which is independent of
n
.
Here,
\operatorname{frac}\left(x\right):=x-\lfloor x\rfloor\in[0,1)
is the fractional part of
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

Let

\mathbb{L}:=\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}\times\mathbb{Z}

and choose any
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},m)\in\mathbb{L}
. Then, the pair
(\widetilde{\gamma}_{\mathbf{I}},{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})
given by

\displaystyle\delta\,\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}:=2\pi\ \operatorname{frac}\left(\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}{2\pi}-mr+\frac{1}{2}\right)-\pi,

(4.6a)

\displaystyle{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=({\mathbf{K}}_{\star}-{\mathbf{K}})\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2}+2\pi m,

(4.6b)

satisfies the two compatibility relations in (
4.4
). Finally, to ensure that
|\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}|=\delta^{-1}\,|\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq 1
, we consider indices
{\mathbf{I}}
that belong to

\mathbb{L}(\delta):=\big\{\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}\ \ \big|\ \ |\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq\delta\mskip 1.5mu\big\}.

(4.7)

The objects
{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}
,
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}
,
\mathbb{L}
and
\mathbb{L}(\delta)
naturally arise and play an important role in Section
6.3
.

Remark 4.2

(The set
\mathbb{L}(\delta)
for rational and irrational edges)
.

As shown in Proposition
A.1
, the set
\mathbb{L}(\delta)
is quite explicit for
r
rational and
\delta
small enough. For instance,
\mathbb{L}(\delta)=\{{\mathbf{K}}\}\times\mathbb{Z}
for
r=0
, and
\mathbb{L}(\delta)=\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}\times\mathbb{Z}
for
r=1
; see Figure
4
. Moreover, for rational edges, the sequence
(\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta)}
takes a finite number of values including
0
, and so
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}=0
for
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta)
, provided that
\delta
is small enough (Proposition
A.1
). On the other hand, if
r
is irrational,
(\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}
is dense in
[-\pi,\pi]
, so one can extract a subsequence which tends to
0
. In particular,
\mathbb{L}(\delta)
is a non-empty, nontrivial, and countable set.

In the following, for any fixed index
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta)
, we assume
\psi_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s,\zeta)
to be of the form (
4.2
), where
\widetilde{\gamma}\equiv\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}
and
{\lambda}={\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}
are given by (
4.6
).

4.2.
Multiscale expansion

Fix
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},m)\in\mathbb{L}(\delta)
, so that
|\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq 1
. Define

\Upsilon^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}(\mu;D_{\zeta}):=\mathrm{i}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\,D_{\zeta}+(\mskip 1.5mu\mu+\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}\mskip 1.5mu)\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\qquad(\textnormal{with}\quad D_{\zeta}:=-\mathrm{i}\,\partial_{\zeta}).

(4.8)

Substituting the ansatz (
4.2
,
4.3
,
4.6
) into (
4.1
), we find that
(E,\psi)\equiv(E_{\mathbf{I}},\psi_{\mathbf{I}})
satisfies the problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ }l}\big(-\Delta_{\bm{x}}+V({\bm{x}})+\delta\,\mathscr{G}^{(1)}+\delta^{2}\,\mathscr{G}^{(2)}+\delta^{3}\,\mathscr{G}^{(3)}\big)\,\psi_{\mathbf{I}}({\bm{x}},\zeta)&=E_{\mathbf{I}}\,\psi_{\mathbf{I}}({\bm{x}},\zeta),\quad({\bm{x}},\zeta)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R},\\[12.0pt]
{\bm{x}}\mapsto\psi_{\mathbf{I}}({\bm{x}},\zeta)\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}},\quad\zeta\mapsto\psi_{\mathbf{I}}({\bm{x}},\zeta)&\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}),\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\big(\mskip 1.5mu2\,\Upsilon^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}(\mu;D_{\zeta})-\kappa(\zeta)\,a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu\big),

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\kappa(\zeta)\,a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\Upsilon^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}(\mu;D_{\zeta})+\Upsilon^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}(\mu;D_{\zeta})\cdot\kappa(\zeta)\,a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}-\Upsilon^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}(\mu;D_{\zeta})\cdot\Upsilon^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}(\mu;D_{\zeta}),

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}^{(3)}

\displaystyle:=\Upsilon^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}(\mu;D_{\zeta})\cdot\kappa(\zeta)\,a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\Upsilon^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}(\mu;D_{\zeta}).

We seek a multiscale expansion of the solution, as in
[
61
,
64
,
111
,
43
]
. In particular, we expand
(E_{\mathbf{I}},\psi_{\mathbf{I}})
in powers of
\delta
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ =\ }l@{\qquad}r@{\ \equiv\ }l}E_{\mathbf{I}}&E_{D}+\delta\mskip 1.5muE^{(1)}+\delta^{2}\mskip 1.5muE^{(2)}+\dots;&E^{(n)}&E^{(n)}_{\mathbf{I}}(\mu),\\[8.0pt]
\psi_{\mathbf{I}}&\psi^{(0)}+\delta\,\psi^{(1)}+\delta^{2}\,\psi^{(2)}+\dots;&\psi^{(n)}({\bm{x}},\zeta)&\psi^{(n)}_{\mathbf{I}}({\bm{x}},\zeta;\mu).\end{array}\right.

By substituting this expansion in the PDE satisfied by
\psi_{\mathbf{I}}
, and by equating terms of same order in powers of
\delta
, we obtain a hierarchy of equations satisfied by
\psi^{(n)}
,
n\geq 0
2
2

2

In the multiscale expansion,
\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}
is treated as a
\mathcal{O}(1)
term, even though its actual order may be higher.
. Each of these equations is completed with the boundary conditions

\displaystyle\forall\;n\geq 0,\quad{\bm{x}}\mapsto\psi^{(n)}({\bm{x}},\zeta)\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}},\quad\zeta\mapsto\psi^{(n)}({\bm{x}},\zeta)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}),

which express
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}
–pseudo-periodicity in
{\bm{x}}
and decay in
\zeta
respectively.

At order
\delta^{0}
and for a fixed
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
, one finds that
\psi^{(0)}(\cdot\,,\zeta)
satisfies

(\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}-E_{D})\,\psi^{(0)}(\cdot\,,\zeta)=0.

Since the kernel of
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}-E_{D}
is a two-dimensional space spanned by the pair
\{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1},\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2}\}
of eigenfunctions associated to
({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},E_{D})
(Condition

(

⁢
i
i

)

in Proposition
2.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\psi^{(0)}({\bm{x}},\zeta)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}(\zeta)\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1}({\bm{x}})+\alpha_{2}(\zeta)\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2}({\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\alpha(\zeta),\quad\text{with}\quad\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}=(\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1},\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2})^{\top},

(4.9)

and where
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})^{\top}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
remains to be determined.

At order
\delta^{1}
, one finds that
\psi^{(1)}(\cdot\,,\zeta)
satisfies

(\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}-E_{D})\,\psi^{(1)}(\cdot\,,\zeta)=(-\mathscr{G}^{(1)}+E^{(1)})\,\psi^{(0)}(\cdot\,,\zeta).

(4.10)

For
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
fixed, (
4.10
) has a solution in
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}
only if its right-hand side is
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}
–orthogonal to the Dirac basis functions
\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{l}
, that is,

\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{l},\,(-\mathscr{G}^{(1)}+E^{(1)})\,\psi^{(0)}(\cdot\,,\zeta)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}=0,\quad l=1,2.

These solvability conditions, combined with the form (
4.9
) of
\psi^{(0)}
and the definition of
\mathscr{G}^{(1)}
, lead to the eigenvalue problem:
find
(E^{(1)},\alpha)\in\mathbb{C}\times L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
such that for
l=1,2
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1,2}\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{l},-2\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{j}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}(D_{\zeta}\mskip 1.5mu\alpha_{j})\mskip 1.5mu(\zeta)+(\mu+\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}})\mskip 1.5mu\sum_{j=1,2}\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{l},-2\mathrm{i}{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{j}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}\alpha_{j}(\zeta)\\
+\kappa(\zeta)\sum_{j=1,2}\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{l},\nabla\cdot\big(a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{j}\big)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}\alpha_{j}(\zeta)-E^{(1)}\,\alpha_{l}(\zeta)=0.

(4.11)

We achieve a simplification of (
4.11
) using the following propositions.

Proposition 4.3

(
[
63
, Eq. (7.28)–(7.29)]
)
.

For
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
and
{\bm{\ell}}=({\bm{\ell}}^{(1)},{\bm{\ell}}^{(2)})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, let

\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{\ell}}):=\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1},-2\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}\cdot\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}&\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1},-2\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}\cdot\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}\\[8.0pt]
\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2},-2\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}\cdot\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}&\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2},-2\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}\cdot\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}\end{pmatrix}.

(4.12)

Then,

\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{\ell}})=\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\begin{pmatrix}0&{\bm{\ell}}^{(1)}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}^{(2)}\\
{\bm{\ell}}^{(1)}-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}^{(2)}&0\end{pmatrix}=\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}^{(1)}\sigma_{1}-{\bm{\ell}}^{(2)}\sigma_{2}\mskip 1.5mu\big).

Here,
\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}}=\upsilon_{D}
and
\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}}=-\upsilon_{D}
, where
\upsilon_{D}>0
is the Dirac velocity (
2.7
); see Condition

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

in Proposition
2.2
.

Proposition 4.4

(
[
43
, Proposition 4.2]
)
.

For
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
,

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1},\nabla\cdot\big(a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1}\big)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}&\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1},\nabla\cdot\big(a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2}\big)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}\\[8.0pt]
\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2},\nabla\cdot\big(a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1}\big)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}&\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2},\nabla\cdot\big(a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2}\big)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}\end{pmatrix}=\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\sigma_{3},

with
\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}}=\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}}:=\left\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}}_{1},\nabla\cdot\big(a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}}_{1}\big)\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}}}\neq 0
under Assumption (

A

.3
) (Section
3.1
).

By Proposition
4.3
(with
{\bm{\ell}}={\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
) and Proposition
4.4
, Equation (
4.11
) becomes

\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\mu+\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}})-E^{(1)}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\alpha(\zeta)=0,

(4.13)

where
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu}):H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
is the
effective Dirac operator
:

\forall\;\widehat{\mu}\in\mathbb{R},\quad\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu}):=\displaystyle\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,D_{\zeta}+\widehat{\mu}\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})+\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\kappa(\zeta)\,\sigma_{3}.

(4.14)

In the next section, Section
4.3
, we show (using the property
\kappa(\zeta)\to\pm 1
as
\zeta\to\pm\infty
) that
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
has a gap in its continuous spectrum centered around
0
, containing at least one isolated eigenvalue, and that all such isolated eigenvalues are simple. As explained in Section
4.4
below, the eigenpairs
(E^{(1)},\alpha(\zeta))
of the effective operators
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\mu+\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}})
give rise to approximate edge states which, at leading order in
\delta
, have the form
\psi^{(0)}({\bm{x}},\zeta)=\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\alpha(\zeta)
, with
\zeta=\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})
.

The order
\delta
correction to
\psi^{(0)}({\bm{x}},\zeta)
is derived as follows. Let
(E^{(1)},\alpha(\zeta))\in\mathbb{R}\times L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
be an eigenpair (
4.13
). Then the right-hand side of (
4.10
) belongs to
P_{\perp}L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}
, where
P_{\perp}
is the
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}
–projector onto the orthogonal complement of
\Ker\,(\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}-E_{D})=\vect\{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1},\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2}\}
. By Fredholm’s alternative (see e.g.
[
26
, Theorem 6.6]
), it follows that
\smash{(\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}-E_{D})|_{P_{\perp}L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}}
has a bounded inverse
R_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\in\mathscr{L}(P_{\perp}L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}})
, and we find that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\psi^{(1)}({\bm{x}},\zeta)

\displaystyle=\psi^{(1)}_{p}({\bm{x}},\zeta)+\psi^{(1)}_{h}({\bm{x}},\zeta),\quad\text{with}\quad

\displaystyle\psi^{(1)}_{p}({\bm{x}},\zeta)

\displaystyle:=R_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\big(-\mathscr{G}^{(1)}+E^{(1)}\big)\,\psi^{(0)}({\bm{x}},\zeta)\quad\text{and}\quad\psi^{(1)}_{h}({\bm{x}},\zeta):=\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\beta(\zeta),

(4.15)

and where
\beta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
is obtained using the solvability conditions of the equation satisfied at order
\delta^{2}
. This multiple scale expansion procedure can be continued to construct approximations of edge states at any arbitrary order
\delta^{n}
,
n\geq 2
.

4.3.
Spectral properties of the effective Dirac operator

The Dirac operator
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
defined by (
4.14
), and which emerges from the multiscale expansion in Section
4.2
, is unitarily equivalent to another Dirac operator studied for instance in
[
43
]
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
,
\widehat{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}
, and introduce the Dirac operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{0}(\widehat{\mu}):=\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,|{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}|\,\sigma_{1}\,D_{\zeta}-\frac{\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}{|{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}|}\,\widehat{\mu}\,\sigma_{2}+\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\kappa(\zeta)\,\sigma_{3},

with
\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}}=\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}}
. There exist constants
(K,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}
depending on
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}
,
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
, and
\upsilon_{D}
only, and with
|\omega|=1
, such that the following unitary equivalence relation holds:

\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})=\mathcal{N}(\widehat{\mu})^{*}\;\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{0}(\widehat{\mu})\;\mathcal{N}(\widehat{\mu}).\quad\textrm{Here,}\quad(\mathcal{N}(\widehat{\mu})\,\alpha)\mskip 1.5mu(\zeta):=\begin{pmatrix}\omega&0\\
0&\overline{\omega}\end{pmatrix}\,\alpha(\zeta)\;\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\widehat{\mu}\mskip 1.5muK\mskip 1.5mu\zeta}.

(4.16)

Proof
.

It suffices to find
(K,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}
for which (
4.16
) holds. Let
{\mathfrak{z}}_{j}:={\mathfrak{K}}^{(1)}_{j}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}^{(2)}_{j}
, with
|{\mathfrak{K}}_{j}|=|{\mathfrak{z}}_{j}|
,
j=1,2
. Using the definition (
4.14
) of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
, the expression for
\mathcal{N}(\widehat{\mu})
, and the constraint
|\omega|^{2}=1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(\widehat{\mu})\,\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})\,\mathcal{N}(\widehat{\mu})^{*}\!\!

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=\begin{pmatrix}0&Z_{1}\\
\overline{Z_{1}}&0\end{pmatrix}\,D_{\zeta}+\widehat{\mu}\,\begin{pmatrix}0&Z_{2}\\
\overline{Z_{2}}&0\end{pmatrix}+\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\kappa(\zeta)\,\sigma_{3},

\displaystyle\quad\text{with}\quad Z_{1}

\displaystyle:=\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\omega^{2}\,{\mathfrak{z}}_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad Z_{2}:=\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\omega^{2}\,({\mathfrak{z}}_{1}-K\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{z}}_{2}).

The right-hand side of the above equation coincides with
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{0}(\widehat{\mu})
if and only if

\displaystyle Z_{1}=\omega^{2}\,\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,{\mathfrak{z}}_{2}=\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,|{\mathfrak{z}}_{2}|\quad\text{and}\quad Z_{2}=\omega^{2}\,\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\mathfrak{z}}_{1}-K\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{z}}_{2})=\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\,\frac{\upsilon^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}{|{\mathfrak{z}}_{2}|}.

(4.17)

The first equation in (
4.17
) determines
\omega
up to sign, with
|\omega|=1
. We substitute this into the second equation in (
4.17
), to obtain

K=\frac{{\mathfrak{z}}_{1}\,\overline{{\mathfrak{z}}_{2}}-\mathrm{i}}{|{\mathfrak{z}}_{2}|^{2}}.

Finally, using the relation

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{z}}_{1}\,\overline{{\mathfrak{z}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}^{(1)}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}^{(2)})\,({\mathfrak{K}}_{2}^{(1)}-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}^{(2)})=({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\wedge{\mathfrak{K}}_{1})

\displaystyle=({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{k}}_{2}\wedge{\mathbf{k}}_{1})=({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})+\mathrm{i}\quad\textnormal{by (\ref{eq:def_edge_vectors}, \ref{eq:honeycomb_vector_quasimomenta})},

we deduce that
K=({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})/|{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}|^{2}
is indeed a real coefficient. Therefore, the proof of Lemma
4.5
is complete.

Thanks to Lemma
4.5
, the spectral properties of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
can be derived from those of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{0}(\widehat{\mu})
. We use the results
[
46
, Lemma 3.1]
and
[
43
, Proposition 4.5]
, on the spectrum of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{0}(\widehat{\mu})
.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
.
Assume
\kappa(\zeta)
is a domain wall function (see Definition
3.1
), and that
\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\neq 0
(see Assumption (

A

.3
)). Then the following holds:

(a).

The essential spectrum of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(0)
coincides with its absolutely continuous part, which is
\mathbb{R}\setminus(-\theta_{\textnormal{gap}},\theta_{\textnormal{gap}})
, with
\theta_{\textnormal{gap}}:=|\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}|
. Moreover, zero is always in the point spectrum, and there exists an integer
N\geq 0
such that the discrete spectrum of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(0)
consists of
2N+1
simple eigenvalues located in the gap
(-\theta_{\textnormal{gap}},\theta_{\textnormal{gap}})
, and symmetric about
0
:

-\theta_{\textnormal{gap}}<z_{-N}<\dots<z_{-1}<z_{0}=0<z_{1}<\dots<z_{N}<\theta_{\textnormal{gap}},\quad z_{-j}=-z_{j}.

(b).

For any
\widehat{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}
, the essential spectrum of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
is purely absolutely continuous, and is given by

\mathbb{R}\setminus(-\theta_{\textnormal{gap}}(\widehat{\mu}),\theta_{\textnormal{gap}}(\widehat{\mu})),\quad\text{where}\quad\theta_{\textnormal{gap}}(\widehat{\mu}):=\sqrt{\theta_{\textnormal{gap}}^{2}+\frac{\upsilon^{2}_{D}}{|{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}|^{2}}\,\widehat{\mu}^{2}}.

The discrete spectrum of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
consists of
2N+1
simple eigenvalues
z_{j}(\widehat{\mu})
,
-N\leq j\leq N
, in the gap
(-\theta_{\textnormal{gap}}(\widehat{\mu}),\theta_{\textnormal{gap}}(\widehat{\mu}))
:

\displaystyle z_{0}(\widehat{\mu}):=\frac{\upsilon_{D}}{|{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}|}\,\widehat{\mu}\,\sign(\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}),\quad z_{\pm j}(\widehat{\mu}):=\pm\sqrt{z^{2}_{j}+\frac{\upsilon^{2}_{D}}{|{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}|^{2}}\,\widehat{\mu}^{2}},\quad\forall\;j=1,\dots,N.

where
z_{j}
,
-N\leq j\leq N
, are the eigenvalues of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(0)
. Moreover, for any
-N\leq j\leq N
, one can find an eigenfunction
\alpha^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{j}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
such that

\forall\;\widehat{\mu}\in\mathbb{R},\quad\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{0}(\widehat{\mu})-z_{j}(\widehat{\mu})\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\alpha^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{j}=0,

that is, eigenfunctions of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{0}(\widehat{\mu})
can be chosen to be independent of
\widehat{\mu}
. Hence, the
\mathcal{N}(\widehat{\mu})^{*}\,\alpha_{j}
,
-N\leq j\leq N
, where
\mathcal{N}(\widehat{\mu})^{*}
is defined in (
4.16
), are eigenfunctions of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
.

The spectrum of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
as a function of
\widehat{\mu}
is plotted in Figure
3
for
N=1
.

Figure 3
.
Spectrum of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
with respect to
\widehat{\mu}
for
N=1
. The gray area represents the essential spectrum, the red curve is the topologically protected zero eigenvalue
\widehat{\mu}\mapsto z_{0}(\widehat{\mu})
, and the green curves represent
\widehat{\mu}\mapsto(z_{-1}(\widehat{\mu}),z_{1}(\widehat{\mu}))
.

Remark 4.7
.

The eigenvalue curve,
\widehat{\mu}\mapsto z_{0}(\widehat{\mu})
, is “topologically protected” in the sense that it persists against arbitrary (even large) spatially localized perturbations of the domain wall function,
\kappa(\zeta)
. Further, while
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
has at least one eigenvalue
z_{0}(\widehat{\mu})
, the total number of eigenvalues depends on the details of
\kappa
. For a prescribed
N\geq 0
, the function
\kappa
can be constructed to ensure that
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
admits exactly
2N+1
eigenvalues; see
[
115
]
.

4.4.
Summary of multiscale procedure and implications for irrational edges

For
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},m)\in\mathbb{L}(\delta)
and
-N\leq j\leq N
, let
(z_{j}(\widehat{\mu}),\,\alpha^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{j}(\cdot\,;\,\widehat{\mu}))\in\mathbb{R}\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
denote an eigenpair of the effective Dirac operator
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
. Then setting
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu
with
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
, the formal analysis of Section
4.2
shows that the function:

\Psi^{\delta,(0)}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{I}},j}:({\bm{x}},s)\mapsto\exp\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\big(\mskip 1.5mu(\mu\,\delta+\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}+({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\mskip 1.5mus\mskip 1.5mu\big)\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\,\\
\times\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\alpha^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{j}\big(\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\,;\,\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\big)

(4.18)

defines a
\mathcal{O}(\delta)
–approximate solution to (
3.12
) associated with the energy
E_{D}+\delta\mskip 1.5muz_{j}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})
.

Evaluating this solution at
s=0
(note that
\alpha^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{j}(\cdot\,;\,\widehat{\mu})\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\subset\mathscr{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})
), leads to the two-dimensional
\mathcal{O}(\delta)
–approximate edge state for the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{\rm dw}
(see (
3.1
)):

\Psi^{\delta,(0)}_{{\mathbf{I}},j,k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}({\bm{x}})=\exp\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\big(\mskip 1.5mu(\mu\,\delta+\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\alpha^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{j}\big(\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{x}}\,;\,\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\big).

(4.19)

For the case of irrational edges, the expression (
4.19
) is a quasiperiodic function as
{\bm{x}}
varies along the line
\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
, and it decays on a long length scale, of order
\delta^{-1}
, in directions transverse to this line.

For rational edges, if
\delta
is small enough, then
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}=0
for
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta)
. Therefore, the expression for the
\mathcal{O}(\delta)
–approximate edge state reduces to
\Psi^{\delta,(0)}_{{\mathbf{I}},j,k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}({\bm{x}})=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\mu\mskip 1.5mu\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\alpha^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{j}(\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{x}}\,;\,\mu)
, which only involves
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\mu)
. This is consistent with the results in
[
61
,
64
,
111
,
43
]
for rational edges. Note that even though the augmented solutions are distinct, their restriction to
s=0
only depends on
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}
. On the other hand, if
r
is irrational, then
\{\delta^{-1}\,\gamma^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}\}_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta)}
forms a nontrivial and dense set in
[-1,1]
, therefore giving rise to countably many approximate
{\mathbf{I}}
–dependent edge states, seeded by the point eigenvalues of a corresponding countable family of effective Dirac operators.

We conclude this section by noting that the procedure in Section
4.2
is not a standard multiple scale expansion in that there is a
\delta
-dependence of the individual terms through
\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}
. Yet it does respect “scale separation”. Specifically, since
|\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq\delta
(
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta)
) the expression (
4.19
) is a slow modulation of
\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})
:

\Psi^{\delta,(0)}_{{\mathbf{I}},j,k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}({\bm{x}})=\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\underbrace{\alpha^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{j}\big(\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{x}}\,;\,\mu+\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}\big)\times\exp\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\big(\mskip 1.5mu(\mu\,+\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}})\,\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\mskip 1.5mu\Big]}_{U\scalebox{0.82}{$\big($}\mskip 1.5mu\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}},\,\delta{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{x}}\,;\,\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}\mskip 1.5mu\scalebox{0.82}{$\big)$}}\,,

(4.20)

where
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}:=\delta\,\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}
and
|\widetilde{\gamma}^{\delta}_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq 1
.

5.
Preliminaries for the analysis of the augmented eigenvalue problem

We reformulate the augmented eigenvalue problem (
3.12
) using an appropriate framework involving Sobolev spaces, and we introduce a Fourier transform for its study.

5.1.
Formulation of the augmented eigenvalue problem in a Sobolev space

Due to their periodicity properties (
3.9
) along the lattice
\mathbb{Z}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{a}}_{1}+\mathbb{Z}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{a}}_{2}
defined by (
3.8
), the coefficients
A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}
,
V_{\textnormal{aug}}
, defined on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, are completely determined by their restriction to the cylinder

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}:=\mathbb{R}^{3}/(\mathbb{Z}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{a}}_{1}+\mathbb{Z}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{a}}_{2}).

Moreover, since
{\mathbf{a}}_{\perp}
is transverse to
\Gamma
, (
3.7
), the cylinder
\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}
may be identified with
\Omega\times\mathbb{R}
in the following trivial manner:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}

\displaystyle\phantom{:}\equiv\big\{\tau_{1}\,{\mathbf{a}}_{1}+\tau_{2}\,{\mathbf{a}}_{2}+s\,{\mathbf{a}}_{\perp}\ \ \big|\ \ \tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in(0,1),\ s\in\mathbb{R}\big\}

\displaystyle:=\big\{(\tau_{1}\,{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+\tau_{2}\,{\mathbf{v}}_{2},\,r\mskip 1.5mu\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}+s)\ \ \big|\ \ \tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in(0,1),\ s\in\mathbb{R}\big\}=\Omega\times\mathbb{R}.

(5.1)

Given
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\in\mathbb{R}
, we shall work with the space of
augmented
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}
–pseudo-periodic
functions:

\displaystyle L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}):=\left\{F\in L^{2}_{\textit{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{r@{\ =\ }l}F({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{1},s+r)&\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5muk_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}\,F({\bm{x}},s)\\[4.0pt]
F({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{2},s-1)&F({\bm{x}},s)\\[4.0pt]
\lx@intercol\hfil s\mapsto F({\bm{x}},s)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}\right.\right\},

(5.2)

which admits the characterization

L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})=\Big\{F\in L^{2}_{\textit{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\ \ \Big|\ \ \mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5muk_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}})}\;F\in L^{2}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})\Big\}.

(5.3)

In particular,
L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}=0}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})=L^{2}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
. Note that
L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
encodes the boundary conditions in (
3.12
). The identification
\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}\equiv\Omega\times\mathbb{R}
justified in (
5.1
) allows us to equip
L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
with the
L^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R})
–inner product and the associated norm.

Similarly, for
q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we set
H^{q=0}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}):=L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
, and inductively define:

\displaystyle\forall\;q\in\mathbb{N},\quad H^{q}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}):=\Big\{F\in H^{q-1}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})\ \ \Big|\ \ \nabla_{\bm{x}}\,F\in[\mskip 1.5muH^{q-1}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})\mskip 1.5mu]^{2}\Big\}.

(5.4)

We equip
H^{q}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
with the
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{s};H^{q}(\Omega))
–inner product and the associated norm. The subscript “
{\bm{x}}
” in
\smash{H^{q}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}
indicates regularity with respect to the variable
{\bm{x}}
alone, which makes it an anisotropic space. Elements of
\smash{H^{q}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}
,
q\geq 1
, can also be characterized via trace (restriction) operators
[
56
,
114
,
1
]
, although their definition can be very tricky due to the anisotropy
[
96
]
.

Finally, we introduce the operator

\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}:=-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}+V_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s),\qquad\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}):=H^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}).

(5.5)

Then, the eigenvalue problem (
3.12
) can be reformulated as:

\displaystyle(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}=0,\qquad\Psi_{\textnormal{aug}}\in\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}).

(5.6)

In other words, augmented edge states are eigenfunctions of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}
.

While not essential for the remainder of this work, the next result is intended to offer more insight into the spectral properties of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}
. This is a direct adaptation of results on almost periodic
[
139
]
and random
[
124
]
Schrödinger operators.

Proposition 5.1
.

(a).

We have:

\forall\;k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\in\mathbb{R},\quad\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}+2\pi}=\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}+2\pi r}=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi\mskip 1.5mus}\,\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi\mskip 1.5mus},

(5.7)

where
\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi\mskip 1.5mus}
is to be understood as a multiplication operator. Therefore:

(b).

If
r=b_{1}/a_{1}
with coprime
(b_{1},a_{1})\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{N}
, then
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}+2\pi/a_{1}})=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}})
.

(c).

If
r
is irrational, then the spectrum of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}
is independent of
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}
.

Proof
.

(a)
.
Equivalence relations
. The operator
{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}}
depends on
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}
only through its domain. Therefore, (
5.7
) follows from the relation
\smash{L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}+2\pi}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})=L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}
and from the fact that
\smash{F\in L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}
if and only if
\smash{\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi\mskip 1.5mus}\,F\in L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}+2\pi r}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}
.

(b)
.
Spectrum for rational edges
. Assume that
r=b_{1}/a_{1}
, with coprime
(a_{1},b_{1})\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{Z}
. Since there exists a pair of coprime integers
(a_{2},b_{2})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
such that
a_{1}\mskip 1.5mub_{2}-a_{2}\mskip 1.5mub_{1}=1
, we get the unitary equivalence relation

\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}+2\pi/a_{1}}=\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}-2\pi\mskip 1.5mua_{2}\mskip 1.5mur+2\pi b_{2}}=\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi\mskip 1.5mua_{2}\mskip 1.5mus}\,\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi\mskip 1.5mua_{2}\mskip 1.5mus}\quad\textnormal{by \eqref{eq:relation_augmented_operator},}

which implies that
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}+2\pi/a_{1}})=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}})
.

(c)
.
Spectrum for irrational edges
. In order to prove that
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}})
is independent of
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}
for irrational edges, it is convenient to work with the operator acting on
L^{2}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\ }l}\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}}}(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}})&:=\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5muk_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}})}\,\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5muk_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}})}\\[8.0pt]
&\phantom{:}=-(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}+\mathrm{i}\,k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu)\cdot A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)\,(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}+\mathrm{i}\,k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu)+V_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s).\end{array}

It follows from (
5.7
) that for any
(m,n)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,

\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}+2\pi(m+nr))=\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi(m+nr)\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi n\mskip 1.5mus}\,\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}})\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi n\mskip 1.5mus}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi(m+nr)\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}.

As a consequence,

\forall\;(m,n)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2},\quad\operatorname{Spec}\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}+2\pi(m+nr))\mskip 1.5mu\big)=\operatorname{Spec}\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}})\mskip 1.5mu\big).

(5.8)

Since
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}}}(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}})
varies polynomially in
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}
, the map
R:k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\mapsto(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}}}(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}})-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu)^{-1}
is continuous from
\mathbb{R}
to
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
. By classical perturbation arguments
[
98
, Theorem V.4.10]
, it follows that
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\in\mathbb{R}\mapsto\operatorname{Spec}(R(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}))
is continuous with respect to the Hausdorff metric. Moreover, by (
5.8
),
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\mapsto\operatorname{Spec}(R(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}))
is constant on the set of periods
2\pi r\mathbb{Z}+2\pi\mathbb{Z}
. Since
2\pi r\mathbb{Z}+2\pi\mathbb{Z}
is dense in
\mathbb{R}
by Kronecker’s theorem
[
84
, Theorem 442]
, it follows that
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\mapsto\operatorname{Spec}(R(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}))
is constant on
\mathbb{R}
. Therefore,
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}}}(k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}))
, or equivalently
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}})
, is independent of
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}
.

5.2.
Completeness of Floquet-Bloch modes in
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})

In this section, the parallel quasi-momentum is set to
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
. The functions

({\bm{x}},s)\mapsto\Phi_{b}({\bm{x}};{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}},\quad(b,{\lambda})\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{R},

satisfy the same pseudo-periodic boundary conditions as elements of
\smash{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}
. The goal of this section is to show that any element of
\smash{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}
is a superposition of such functions; see Proposition
5.3
. To this end, a natural tool is the
Fourier transform with respect to
s
.

It can be seen from (
5.2
) that
\smash{F\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}
if and only if
\smash{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{K}}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\;F\in L^{2}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}
. Assuming that
\smash{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{K}}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\;F\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}
, we can then introduce the partial Fourier transform:

(\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F)({\bm{x}},{\lambda})=\widetilde{F}({\bm{x}},{\lambda}):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}F({\bm{x}},s)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,ds,\quad({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}.

(5.9)

From its definition,
{\lambda}\mapsto\widetilde{F}({\bm{x}},{\lambda})
is the Fourier transform of
s\mapsto F({\bm{x}},s)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\mskip 1.5mus}
, where the term
\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\mskip 1.5mus}
was added simply for convenience. To study the properties of
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
, introduce the space

\displaystyle L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}):=\big\{\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{F}\in L^{2}_{\textit{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\ \ \big|\ \ \widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}},\ \widetilde{F}({\bm{x}},\cdot)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})\mskip 1.5mu\big\},

(5.10)

which is equipped with the
L^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R})
–norm. Similarly, for any
q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, define

\displaystyle L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}):=\big\{\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{F}\in L^{2}_{\textit{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\ \ \big|\ \ \widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\in H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}},\ \widetilde{F}({\bm{x}},\cdot)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})\mskip 1.5mu\big\},

(5.11)

equipped with the
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};H^{q}(\Omega))
–norm. We emphasize that
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})
is anisotropic, as its definition involves derivatives with respect to the variable
{\bm{x}}
only. We next show that the transform
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
defines an isomorphism from
H^{q}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})
for any
q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and we provide some of its properties.

Proposition 5.2

(Properties of
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
)
.

(a).

The transform
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
, defined in (
5.9
) for smooth functions, extends by density to a continuous mapping from
L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})
.

(b).

The adjoint of
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
is the operator
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}^{*}:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})\to L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
defined for any
G\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})
as

(\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}^{*}\,G)({\bm{x}},s):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}G({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,d{\lambda},\quad({\bm{x}},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}.

(5.12)

(c).

We have the Plancherel-like identity:
