\forall\;F,G\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),\quad\left\langle\mskip 1.5muG,\,F\mskip 1.5mu\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}G(\cdot\,,{\lambda}),\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}}\!d{\lambda}.

(5.13)

(d).

We have the relations:

\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}^{*}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}={\operatorname{Id}}_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})},\quad\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}^{*}={\operatorname{Id}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})}.

(5.14)

(e).

The transform
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
commutes with multiplication by functions constant in
s
: given a function
p\in\mathscr{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2}/\Lambda)
identified with its extension
({\bm{x}},s)\mapsto p({\bm{x}})
, we have

\displaystyle\forall\;F\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),\quad\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,(p\,F)\,({\bm{x}},{\lambda})=p({\bm{x}})\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F({\bm{x}},{\lambda}),\quad({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}.

(5.15)

(f).

The transform
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
commutes with differential operators in
{\bm{x}}
:

\forall\;F\in H^{1}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),\quad\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\mskip 1.5muF=\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,F.

(5.16)

In particular,
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
is isomorphic from
H^{q}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})
for any
q\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof
.

Point
(a)
. Continuity of
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
. Let
F\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
with
\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{K}}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\;F\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
. We first prove that
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F(\cdot\,,{\lambda})
is
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
–pseudo-periodic with respect to the triangular lattice
\Lambda=\mathbb{Z}{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+\mathbb{Z}{\mathbf{v}}_{2}
for any
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
. On one hand, using the relations

F({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{1},s+r)=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}\,F({\bm{x}},s),\quad{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}=({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}={\mathbf{v}}_{1}+r\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{v}}_{2},

we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{1},{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}F({\bm{x}},s-r)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,ds

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot r{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}F({\bm{x}},s)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,ds

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{1}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F({\bm{x}},{\lambda}).

On the other hand, the property
F({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{2},s-1)=F({\bm{x}},s)
leads to

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F({\bm{x}}+{\mathbf{v}}_{2},{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}F({\bm{x}},s+1)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,ds=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F({\bm{x}},{\lambda}).

Consequently,
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}
for any
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
. Furthermore, by the Plancherel formula for the one-dimensional Fourier transform in
s
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}}|F({\bm{x}},s)|^{2}\,ds=\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F({\bm{x}},{\lambda})|^{2}\,d{\lambda},\quad{\bm{x}}\in\Omega.

Integrating this relation over
{\bm{x}}\in\Omega
yields
\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R})}=\|\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R})}
, which is a continuity estimate for smooth functions. Since
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
is dense in
L^{2}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
, it follows that
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
extends uniquely as a continuous map from
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})
.

Point
(b)
–
(e)
The expression (
5.12
) for the adjoint
\mathscr{F}^{*}_{{\mathbf{K}}}
follows directly from the definition of the adjoint of the one-dimensional Fourier transform in
s
. (
5.13
) follows by applying the Plancherel formula for the one-dimensional Fourier transform in
s
to
F({\bm{x}},\cdot),G({\bm{x}},\cdot)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and by integrating the result over
{\bm{x}}\in\Omega
. The relations (
5.14
) follow from the unitarity nature of the one-dimensional Fourier transform in
s
. The commutation property (
5.15
) is direct for smooth functions; it extends to
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
by density of
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
in
L^{2}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
.

Point
(d)
. Although (
5.16
) can be easily verified for smooth functions, its proof in the general case requires more care. For
F\in H^{1}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
, we first show that (
5.16
) holds in a weak sense. For any test function
\widetilde{G}({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R})
, derivation in the sense of distributions combined with
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\mskip 1.5muF\in L^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R})
leads to

\displaystyle\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{G},\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\mskip 1.5muF\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{\lambda})]^{\prime},\mskip 1.5mu\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{\lambda})}

\displaystyle:=-\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\widetilde{G},\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\mskip 1.5muF\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{\lambda})]^{\prime},\mskip 1.5mu\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{\lambda})}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\widetilde{G}({\bm{x}},{\lambda})}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\mskip 1.5muF({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\,d{\bm{x}}d{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}^{*}\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\widetilde{G}({\bm{x}},s)}\,F({\bm{x}},s)\,d{\bm{x}}ds,

by the Plancherel-like formula (
5.13
) and the relations (
5.14
). Since
\widetilde{G}
is a smooth function, the integral involved in the definition (
5.12
) of
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}^{*}
can be interchanged with
\nabla_{\bm{x}}
, to obtain
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}^{*}\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\widetilde{G}=\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}^{*}\,\widetilde{G}
. As a consequence,

\displaystyle\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{G},\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\mskip 1.5muF\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{\lambda})]^{\prime},\mskip 1.5mu\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{\lambda})}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}^{*}\widetilde{G}({\bm{x}},s)}\,F({\bm{x}},s)\,d{\bm{x}}ds

\displaystyle=-\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}^{*}\widetilde{G},\,F\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{s})]^{\prime},\mskip 1.5mu\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{s})}

\displaystyle=:\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}^{*}\,\widetilde{G},\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,F\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{s})]^{\prime},\mskip 1.5mu\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{s})},

where the last equality is obtained using derivation in the sense of distributions. Given that
\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,F({\bm{x}},s)\in L^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R})
, the above duality product can be written as an integral over
\Omega\times\mathbb{R}
. Then, reusing the Plancherel-like formula (
5.13
) and the relations (
5.14
), we obtain

\displaystyle\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{G},\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\mskip 1.5muF\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{\lambda})]^{\prime},\mskip 1.5mu\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{\lambda})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\widetilde{G}({\bm{x}},{\lambda})}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,F({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\,d{\bm{x}}d{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{G},\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,F\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{\lambda})]^{\prime},\mskip 1.5mu\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{\lambda})}.

In other words, (
5.16
) holds in the sense of distributions. But since
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}F\in L^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R})
, it follows that
\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\mskip 1.5muF\in L^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R})
, and (
5.16
) holds in
L^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R})
.

Proposition
5.2
and the completeness of the Floquet-Bloch modes
\{\Phi_{b}(\cdot\,;{\mathbf{k}})\}_{b\geq 1}
in
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{k}}}
for any
{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathscr{B}
lead directly to the following result.

Proposition 5.3

(Completeness of Floquet-Bloch modes in
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
)
.

(a).

Any
F\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
can be expressed as a continuous superposition of Floquet-Bloch modes with quasi-momenta located on the line
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
:

\displaystyle F({\bm{x}},s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{F}({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,d{\lambda}

(5.17a)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{b\geq 1}\widetilde{F}_{b}({\lambda})\,\Phi_{b}({\bm{x}};{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,d{\lambda},\quad\text{for}\quad({\bm{x}},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}.

(5.17b)

Here,

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{b}({\lambda})

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\big\langle\Phi_{b}(\cdot\,;{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}),\,\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}}\quad\text{for}\quad(b,{\lambda})\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{R}.

(5.17c)

(b).

The estimate (
2.5
) combined with the properties of
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
in Proposition
5.2
provides the following characterization for any
q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
F\in H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
:

\|\mskip 1.5muF\mskip 1.5mu\|^{2}_{H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}=\|\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{F}\mskip 1.5mu\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})}\hyperref@@ii[item:notation_lesssim_simeq]{\simeq}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{b\geq 1}(1+b^{2})^{q/2}\,|\widetilde{F}_{b}({\lambda})|^{2}\,d{\lambda}.

(5.18)

(c).

Recall that
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\mathbf{k}}=-\Delta+V({\bm{x}})
acts on
L^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}
for any
{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathscr{B}
. For any
F\in H^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
and for
({\bm{x}},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}\,F({\bm{x}},s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\big]({\bm{x}})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,d{\lambda}

(5.19)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{b\geq 1}E_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,\widetilde{F}_{b}({\lambda})\,\Phi_{b}({\bm{x}};{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,d{\lambda}.

(5.20)

In particular,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}})=\big\{\mskip 1.5muE_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}),\ {\lambda}\in\mathbb{R},\ b\geq 1\mskip 1.5mu\big\}.

6.
Edge slices

Following the completeness property stated in Proposition
5.3
, we give particular attention to the
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
–band dispersion functions
, defined as

{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}\mapsto E_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}),\quad b\geq 1.

(6.1)

We refer to their graphs as
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
–band dispersion slices
.

6.1.
Behavior of band dispersion slices in the Brillouin zone

By periodicity, restricting the dispersion functions
{\mathbf{k}}\mapsto E_{b}({\mathbf{k}})
to the line
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
is equivalent to restricting them to the broken line

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}:=({\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}+\Lambda^{*})\cap\mathscr{B}.

In Figure
4
, we plot the truncation of
\mathscr{D}
, obtained from
({\mathbf{K}}+[-L,L]\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}+\Lambda^{*})\cap\mathscr{B}
for
L\gg 1
, for different choices of edges: the zigzag edge (
r=0
, first panel), the armchair edge (
r=1
, second panel), a rational edge (
r=1/2
, third panel), and an irrational edge (
r=\sqrt{2}
, fourth panel). These panels show different behaviors for rational and irrational edges.

(a)

Zigzag
(r=0)

(b)

Armchair
(r=1)

(c)

r=0.5

(d)

r=\sqrt{2}

Figure 4
.
The truncated broken line
({\mathbf{K}}+[-L,L]\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}+\Lambda^{*})\cap\mathscr{B}
for
L=20
.

If
r=b_{1}/a_{1}
is rational, then the broken line
\mathscr{D}
has a finite number of components. Moreover, the function
{\lambda}\mapsto E_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})
is
2\pi\mskip 1.5mua_{1}
–periodic, and therefore can be reconstructed from its values on the interval
[-\pi\mskip 1.5mua_{1},\pi\mskip 1.5mua_{1}]
. If
r
is irrational, then
{\lambda}\mapsto E_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})
is no longer periodic, and Figure
4
suggests that
\mathscr{D}
is dense in
\mathscr{B}
. Indeed, we have

Theorem 6.1

(Kronecker’s theorem,
[
84
, Theorem 442]
)
.

Let
r
be irrational and recall that
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}:=-r{\mathbf{k}}_{1}+{\mathbf{k}}_{2}
. Then for any
{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, the set
{\mathbf{k}}+\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}+\Lambda^{*}
is dense in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. In particular, the broken line
\mathscr{D}
comes arbitrarily close to all vertices of the Brillouin zone
\mathscr{B}
, the union of the two high-symmetry sublattices.

6.2.
The spectral no-fold condition

A key assumption in our forthcoming analysis is that the
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
– slices should be bounded away from the Dirac energy,
E_{D}
, when
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
lies outside a neighborhood of the union of sublattices
\mathbb{K}:=({\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*})\cup({\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*})
generated by the high-symmetry quasi-momenta. For rational edges, this requirement appears in earlier works
[
61
,
111
,
60
,
46
,
43
]
, and is ensured by the
spectral no-fold condition at energy
E_{D}
along the
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
–slice
:

\displaystyle\forall\;{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R},\qquad\exists\;b\geq 1,\ \ E_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})=E_{D}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ {\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\in\mathbb{K}:=({\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*})\cup({\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*}).

(6.2)

The spectral no-fold condition (
6.2
) ensures that
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
–band dispersion slices that intersect the Dirac energy
E_{D}
do so at high-symmetry quasi-momenta only. It can be verified explicitly that (
6.2
) holds for the two-band tight-binding model of graphene, as well as for
the honeycomb Schrödinger operator
-\Delta+g^{2}\,V({\bm{x}})
in the
strong binding regime
(
g\gg 1
). Indeed, in the latter setting it was shown in
[
60
, Theorem 6.1]
that the lowest two bands, after appropriate scaling, converge uniformly to those of the two-band tight-binding model.

For rational edges, the property (
6.2
) implies that the slices
{\lambda}\mapsto E_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})
are bounded away from
E_{D}
whenever
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
is not close to
\mathbb{K}
(see
[
46
,
43
]
): more precisely, for
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\operatorname{dist}\big({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2},\mathbb{K}\big)\geq\varepsilon\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ |E_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})-E_{D}|\geq C_{\varepsilon},\quad b\geq 1.

(6.3)

In contrast, for irrational edges, (
6.2
) does not necessarily imply (
6.3
), due to the dense nature of the broken quasi-momentum line
\mathscr{D}
in the Brillouin zone. In fact, assume that there exist
b\in\{b_{\star},b_{\star}+1\}
and a quasi-momentum
\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}}\in\mathscr{B}\setminus(\mathscr{D}\cup\mathbb{K})
such that
E_{b}(\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}})=E_{D}
3
3

3

Note that the existence of such
\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}}
does not contradict (
6.2
), since
\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}}\not\in\mathscr{D}
.
. By Kronecker’s theorem, there exists a sequence
({\lambda}_{n})_{n>0}
such that
\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}_{n}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2},\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}}+\Lambda^{*})\to 0
. Therefore, since
\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}}
is not a high-symmetry quasi-momentum,
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}_{n}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
is bounded away from
\mathbb{K}
for
n
large enough. However, we have
E_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}_{n}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\to E_{b}(\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}})=E_{D}
from the continuity of the dispersion functions. In conclusion, for irrational edges, the
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
–slices can be arbitrarily close to
E_{D}
even for quasi-momenta
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
,
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
, located far from the high-symmetry quasi-momenta. To ensure that they remain bounded away from
E_{D}
, we need a stronger condition.

Definition 6.2

(Omnidirectional no-fold condition)
.

Let
V
be a honeycomb lattice potential as in Definition
2.1
, and suppose that
\mathcal{H}^{0}:=-\Delta+V({\bm{x}})
has a Dirac point
({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},E_{D})
in the sense of Section
2.5
.
\mathcal{H}^{0}
is said to satisfy the
omnidirectional spectral no-fold condition at energy
E_{D}
if

\displaystyle\forall\;{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\qquad\exists\;b\geq 1,\ \ E_{b}({\mathbf{k}})=E_{D}\ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ {\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{K}:=({\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*})\cup({\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*}).

(6.4)

The omnidirectional spectral no-fold condition (
6.4
) prescribes the behavior of the dispersion
surfaces
, instead of a particular
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
–slice. We show in Lemma
8.4
that if (
6.4
) holds, then the
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
–band dispersion slices
{\lambda}\mapsto E_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})
remain bounded away from
E_{D}
whenever
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
is not near
\mathbb{K}
. Thus, while the
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
–no-fold property (
6.2
) is well-suited for rational edges, the omnidirectional no-fold property (
6.4
) allows to study irrational edges.

In contrast to (
6.2
), the condition (
6.4
) is not edge-dependent. In fact assuming the omnidirectional no-fold condition (
6.4
) is equivalent to assuming that (
6.2
) is satisfied for
all
edges. Hence, (
6.4
) is a more restrictive assumption. That said, the omnidirectional no-fold property (
6.4
) holds for the discrete two-band tight-binding model of graphene, and this property continues to the strong binding regime of continuum honeycomb Schrödinger operators
[
60
, Theorem 6.1]
.

6.3.
Neighborhoods of high-symmetry quasi-momenta

We take
r
to be irrational. As in previous work devoted to rational edges
[
61
,
111
,
46
,
43
]
, our goal is construct edge states comprised dominantly of Floquet-Bloch modes
\{\Phi_{b}(\cdot\,;{\mathbf{k}})\}_{b\geq 1}
with quasi-momenta
{\mathbf{k}}={\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
located near the high-symmetry quasi-momenta,
\mathbb{K}:=({\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*})\cup({\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*})
. Recall from Proposition
5.3
that the family of Floquet-Bloch modes with quasi-momenta parameterized by the mapping
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}\mapsto{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
is complete in
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
. Hence, we next characterize those
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
for which
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
is located in some specified small neighborhood of
\mathbb{K}
.

Since
r
is irrational, Theorem
6.1
(see also Figure
4
) implies that there are points on the line
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
which are arbitrarily close to points of both the
{\mathbf{K}}
and
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}
–sublattices. Thus we treat
{\mathbf{K}}
and
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}
–type points equivalently, unless the distinction is specifically needed. We introduce the countable set

\mathbb{L}:=\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}\times\mathbb{Z}.

(6.5)

A generic element of
\mathbb{L}
is denoted
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}},m_{\mathbf{I}})
.

We find it natural to introduce open (parallelogram-shaped) neighborhoods in
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}
:

\forall\;0<\varepsilon\leq\pi,\quad\widehat{\Omega}_{\varepsilon}:=\big\{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+\mathrm{y}_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\ /\ |\mathrm{y}_{j}|<\varepsilon\mskip 1.5mu\big\},\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\Omega}=\widehat{\Omega}_{\pi}.

(6.6)

Note that
\widehat{\Omega}
is a fundamental parallelogram of the lattice
\mathbb{Z}\mskip 1.5mu(2\pi{\mathfrak{K}}_{1})+\mathbb{Z}\mskip 1.5mu(2\pi{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})
, with centering at
\bf{0}
.

We introduce the open set,
B_{\varepsilon}
, consisting of
\varepsilon
–neighborhoods of all points in the union of high-symmetry quasi-momenta sublattices
\mathbb{K}=({\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*})\cup({\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*})
; see Figure
6
:

\forall\;\varepsilon\in(0,1),\quad B_{\varepsilon}:=B^{{\mathbf{K}}}_{\varepsilon}\cup B^{{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}}_{\varepsilon}\quad\text{where}\quad B^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{\varepsilon}:={\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\widehat{\Omega}_{\varepsilon}+\Lambda^{*},\quad\forall\;{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}.

(6.7)

The shape of these neighborhoods has the desirable property that
\{{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}\ /\ {\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\in B^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{\varepsilon}\}
consists of intervals of
equal
length
\varepsilon
. To introduce the centers of these
\varepsilon
–sized intervals, we begin with the following lemma, which shows that any quasi-momentum of the form
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
coincides with a unique element of
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\widehat{\Omega}
, up to translations by elements of the dual lattice
\Lambda^{*}
. Recall that
\operatorname{frac}\left({\lambda}\right):={\lambda}-\lfloor{\lambda}\rfloor\in[0,1)
denotes the fractional part of
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma 6.3

(Wrapping Lemma)
.

Let
r
be irrational, and consider the infinite line in
\mathbb{R}^{2}:{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
.
Here,

{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}=-r{\mathbf{k}}_{1}+{\mathbf{k}}_{2}
and
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}={\mathbf{k}}_{1}
.

(a).

Fix
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
. For any
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
, there is a unique pair:

{\bm{\ell}}({\lambda})=\mathrm{y}_{1}({\lambda})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+\mathrm{y}_{2}({\lambda})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\in\widehat{\Omega}\quad\textrm{(with $|\mathrm{y}_{j}|\leq\pi$) and}\quad{\mathbf{l}}({\lambda})\in\Lambda^{*},

(6.8)

depending on
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}
, such that

{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}({\lambda})+{\mathbf{l}}({\lambda}).

(6.9)

The vector
{\bm{\ell}}({\lambda})
represents the relative position of
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
within the local coordinate system centered at
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}
and aligned with the lattice
\mathbb{Z}\mskip 1.5mu(2\pi{\mathfrak{K}}_{1})+\mathbb{Z}\mskip 1.5mu(2\pi{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})
, up to translation by an element of
\Lambda^{*}
(which is
{\mathbf{l}}({\lambda})
); see Figure
5
.

(b).

For any
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
, the mapping
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\mapsto{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}({\lambda})
, where
{\bm{\ell}}({\lambda})
is given by (
6.8
), is a bijection between the line
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
and a countable union of segments which is dense in
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\widehat{\Omega}
(see Figure
5
). We label these segments with indices:
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},m)\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}\times\mathbb{Z}=\mathbb{L}
, and they have parameterizations:

{\lambda}\in{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+[-\pi,\pi)\mapsto{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda}),

where

{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda}):=\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\in\widehat{\Omega},

(6.10)

and where

\begin{array}[]{r@{\ :=\ }l}{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}&({\mathbf{K}}_{\star}-{\mathbf{K}})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}+2\pi\mskip 1.5mum,\\[8.0pt]
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}&\displaystyle 2\pi\,\operatorname{frac}\left(\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}{2\pi}-m\mskip 1.5mur+\frac{1}{2}\right)-\pi\in[-\pi,\pi).\end{array}

(6.11)

The mid-point of the
{\mathbf{I}}
–indexed segment is the point
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}
, which is attained at
{\lambda}={\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}
.

(c).

As a subset of the infinite line
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}

the
{\mathbf{I}}
–indexed segment in
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\widehat{\Omega}
is given by

{\lambda}\in{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+[-\pi,\pi]\mapsto{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda}),

(6.12)

where

{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})={\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}=\displaystyle 2\pi\left\lfloor\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}{2\pi}-m\mskip 1.5mur+\frac{1}{2}\right\rfloor\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{1}+2\pi\mskip 1.5mum\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{2}\in\Lambda^{*}.

(6.13)

(d).

The relation (
6.12
), namely
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}
, extends to all
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
. However,
{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})
might not belong to
\widehat{\Omega}
if
{\lambda}\not\in{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+[-\pi,\pi)
.

Figure 5
.
Illustration of the Wrapping Lemma
6.3
with
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}={\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}
;
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}
, where

{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})
is in the fundamental cell
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\widehat{\Omega}
. Here,
{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}=2\pi\,(3\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{1}+3\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{2})
and
{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})
is the relative position of
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
in
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\widehat{\Omega}
up to translation by
{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}
. Recall the relations (
3.5
), e.g. that
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}={\mathbf{k}}_{1}
.

Proof
.

Our proof of the Part

(
a
)

of Lemma
6.3
is constructive. Recall that by Kronecker’s theorem, the set
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}+\Lambda^{*}
restricted to
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\widehat{\Omega}
is a countable union of line segments which is dense in
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\widehat{\Omega}
(where
\widehat{\Omega}
is defined by (
6.6
)). Let us now parameterize all such line segments in
\widehat{\Omega}
.

Fix
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
{\bm{\ell}}:=\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+\mathrm{y}_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\in\widehat{\Omega}
and
{\mathbf{l}}=2\pi\mskip 1.5mun_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{1}+2\pi\mskip 1.5mun_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{2}\in\Lambda^{*}
. The constraint that
{\bm{\ell}}
lie in
\widehat{\Omega}
implies that
-\pi\leq\mathrm{y}_{j}<\pi
. Part
(a)
is then equivalent to
finding
\mathrm{y}_{j}\in[-\pi,\pi)
and
n_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
j=1,2
, such that

{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+\mathrm{y}_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}+2\pi\mskip 1.5mun_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{1}+2\pi\mskip 1.5mun_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{2}.

(6.14)

Taking the scalar product with the vectors
{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}
, and using the relations
{\mathfrak{K}}_{n}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{l}=\delta_{nl}
as well as
{\mathbf{k}}_{1}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}={\mathbf{k}}_{2}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}=1
,
{\mathbf{k}}_{1}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}=0
, and
{\mathbf{k}}_{2}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}=r
, the above equation reduces to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ =\ }l}{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}&{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\mathrm{y}_{1}+2\pi\,(n_{1}+n_{2}\mskip 1.5mur),\\[8.0pt]
{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}+{\lambda}&{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}+\mathrm{y}_{2}+2\pi\mskip 1.5mun_{2}.\end{array}\right.

(6.15)

Let us note that each equation in (
6.15
) boils down to the toy problem:

\textit{Given $a\in\mathbb{R}$, \hskip 10.22217ptfind $(\mathrm{y},n)\in[-\pi,\pi)\times\mathbb{Z}$ such that}\quad\mathrm{y}+2\pi n=a,

(6.16a)

which may be rewritten as
\mathrm{y}/(2\pi)+1/2=a/(2\pi)+1/2-n
. Taking the integer and fractional parts, we find that the unique solution
(\mathrm{y},n)\in[-\pi,\pi)\times\mathbb{Z}
is given by:

\mathrm{y}=2\pi\,\operatorname{frac}\left(\frac{a}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2}\right)-\pi\quad\text{and}\quad n=\left\lfloor\frac{a}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2}\right\rfloor.

(6.16b)

Applying (
6.16
) to the second equation of (
6.15
) with
a:=({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}+{\lambda}
, we get

\mathrm{y}_{2}({\lambda})=2\pi\,\operatorname{frac}\left(\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}+{\lambda}}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2}\right)-\pi,\quad n_{2}({\lambda})=\left\lfloor\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}+{\lambda}}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2}\right\rfloor.

(6.17)

Next, substitute the expression (
6.17
) for
n_{2}\equiv n_{2}({\lambda})
into the first equation of (
6.15
) and apply (
6.16
) with
a=({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}-2\pi\mskip 1.5mun_{2}({\lambda})\mskip 1.5mur
, to obtain:

\displaystyle\mathrm{y}_{1}({\lambda})

\displaystyle=2\pi\,\operatorname{frac}\left(\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}{2\pi}-n_{2}({\lambda})\mskip 1.5mur+\frac{1}{2}\right)-\pi,

(6.18)

\displaystyle n_{1}({\lambda})

\displaystyle=\left\lfloor\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}{2\pi}-n_{2}({\lambda})\mskip 1.5mur+\frac{1}{2}\right\rfloor.

Thus we have a representation of any point of
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
as the sum of a point in
\widehat{\Omega}
and a point in
\Lambda^{*}
, in terms of parameters
\mathrm{y}_{j}\in[-\pi,\pi)
and
n_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}
. The concludes the proof of Part

(
a
)

.

Turning to Part

(
b
)

, we note that the center of the line segment:
{\lambda}\mapsto{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}({\lambda})\in{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\widehat{\Omega}
occurs for
{\lambda}=\widetilde{{\lambda}}
, such that
\mathrm{y}_{2}(\widetilde{{\lambda}})=0
. From (
6.17
), this implies

\widetilde{{\lambda}}\in({\mathbf{K}}_{\star}-{\mathbf{K}})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}+2\pi\mskip 1.5mu\mathbb{Z}.

For each
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define

{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}:=({\mathbf{K}}_{\star}-{\mathbf{K}})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}+2\pi\mskip 1.5mum,\quad{\rm where}\quad{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},m).

(6.19)

Note that

\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}+{\lambda}}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2}=\frac{{\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2}+m\ \ \in[0,1)+m,

with the latter inclusion holding if
{\lambda}\in{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+[-\pi,\pi)
.
Hence, the expressions (
6.17
) for
(\mathrm{y}_{2},n_{2})
reduce to:

\mathrm{y}_{2}({\lambda})={\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}\quad\text{and}\quad n_{2}=m,

(6.20a)

and further, the expressions for
(\mathrm{y}_{1},n_{1})
, become:

\displaystyle\mathrm{y}_{1}({\lambda})

\displaystyle=\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}:=2\pi\mskip 1.5mu\operatorname{frac}\left(\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}{2\pi}-m\mskip 1.5mur+\frac{1}{2}\right)-\pi,

(6.20b)

\displaystyle n_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\lfloor\frac{({\mathbf{K}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\star})\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}{2\pi}-m\mskip 1.5mur+\frac{1}{2}\right\rfloor,

(6.20c)

where
{\lambda}\in{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+[-\pi,\pi)
. We conclude that the
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},m)
–indexed segment in
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\widehat{\Omega}
is given by

{\lambda}\in{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+[-\pi,\pi)\mapsto{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda}),\quad\text{where}\quad{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})=\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\in\widehat{\Omega}.

This concludes the proof of Part

(
b
)

of Lemma
6.3
.

Part

(
c
)

of Lemma
6.3
follows from the decomposition

{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}},\quad{\rm for}\quad{\lambda}\in{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+[-\pi,\pi),

where

{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}:=2\pi\mskip 1.5mun_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{1}+2\pi\mskip 1.5mun_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{2}\in\Lambda^{*},\quad\text{for}\quad{\lambda}\in{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+[-\pi,\pi),

(6.21)

with
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
as in (
6.20
). Finally, Part

(
d
)

can be verified from direct computations. Thus the proof of the Wrapping Lemma
6.3
is complete.

Figure 6
.
The neighborhoods
B^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{\varepsilon}
restricted to the dual periodicity cell
\Omega^{*}=\{\mskip 1.5muz_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{1}+z_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}_{2}\ /\ z_{1},z_{2}\in(0,2\pi)\mskip 1.5mu\}
, with
r=-\sqrt{2}
. The set of gray lines represents the set
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\mod\Omega^{*}
, which is dense in
\Omega^{*}
. The set of green segments represents the subset of
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\mod\Omega^{*}
that is within
B^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{\varepsilon}
.

By Part

(
d
)

of Lemma
6.3
,
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}){\mathfrak{K}}_{2}+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}
, and so the coordinate
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}
controls displacement along
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}={\mathbf{k}}_{1}
and
{\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}
, displacement along
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
. A consequence is a characterization of segments in
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
within the
\varepsilon
–neighborhood
B_{\varepsilon}
, defined in (
6.7
). The simplification of this result for rational edges is presented in Section
A.1
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and define

\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon):=\left\{\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}\ \ |\ \ |\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq\varepsilon\mskip 1.5mu\right\},

(6.22)

where
\mathbb{L}(\pi)=\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}\times\mathbb{Z}=\mathbb{L}
, and where
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\in[-\pi,\pi)
is the coefficient defined by (
6.20b
). Then, the set of reals
{\lambda}
such that
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\in B_{\varepsilon}
is a countable union of
2\mskip 1.5mu\varepsilon
–sized segments centered at the points
{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}
defined in (
6.19
), for
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon)
:

\textnormal{For}\ {\lambda}\in\mathbb{R},\quad{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\in B_{\varepsilon}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad{\lambda}\in\bigcup_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon)}{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\ .

(6.23)

Further, if
\varepsilon<1/3
, then the union of intervals on the right-hand side of (
6.23
) is a disjoint union.

Proof
.

Fix
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
. For any
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists a unique
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, with
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},m)
, such that
{\lambda}\in{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+[-\pi,\pi)
. It follows from Part

(
b
)

of the Wrapping Lemma
6.3
that
{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda}):=\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
and
{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}
are the unique vectors in
\widehat{\Omega}
and
\Lambda^{*}
respectively, such that
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}
. Therefore, using the definitions of
{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})
and
B^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{\varepsilon}
, we deduce that
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
belongs to
B^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{\varepsilon}
if and only if
|\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq\varepsilon
and
|{\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq\varepsilon
. Using this conclusion for
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}={\mathbf{K}}
and
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}={\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}
, we deduce the characterization (
6.23
).

7.
Statement of the main result, Theorem
7.1
on the resolvent expansion

In this section, we state and discuss the main result of this paper, Theorem
7.1
, on the asymptotic expansion of the resolvent of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu}
as
\delta\to 0
. For what follows, we recall:

(\mathbf{H}.1)

V
is a honeycomb potential in the sense of Definition
2.1
, and
\mathcal{H}^{0}=-\Delta+V({\bm{x}})
admits a Dirac point
({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},E_{D})
in the sense of Section
2.5
.

(\mathbf{H}.2)

{\bm{x}}\mapsto a({\bm{x}})
satisfies the conditions (

A

.1
)–(

A

.3
) in Section
3.1
, namely,
a({\bm{x}})
is smooth,
\Lambda
–periodic, real-valued, even, and
\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}:=\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1},\nabla\cdot(a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1})\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}}\neq 0
, where
\{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1},\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2}\}
is the Dirac eigenbasis introduced in Part

(

⁢
i
i

)

of Proposition
2.2
.

(\mathbf{H}.3)

\mathcal{H}^{0}=-\Delta+V({\bm{x}})
satisfies the omnidirectional no-fold condition at the Dirac energy
E_{D}
; see Definition
6.2
.

Motivated by the multiscale study in Section
4
we begin by introducing the operators involved in the formulation of Theorem
7.1
.

For any
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}},m_{\mathbf{I}})\in\mathbb{L}
and
\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}=(\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}_{1},\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}_{2})^{\top}
, define

\varphi_{\mathbf{I}}({\bm{x}},s):=\exp\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\big(\mskip 1.5mu\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}+({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\mskip 1.5mus\mskip 1.5mu\big)\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}}),\quad({\bm{x}},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R},

(7.1)

the bounded linear operator
F({\bm{x}},s)\mapsto(\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F)(\zeta)
, which averages over
\Omega
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}:L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}),

\displaystyle(\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F)(\zeta):=\int_{\Omega}\mskip 1.5mu\overline{\varphi_{\mathbf{I}}({\bm{x}},\zeta-{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{x}})}\,F\mskip 1.5mu\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{x}},\zeta-{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{x}})\,d{\bm{x}},\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{R},

(7.2)

and its bounded linear adjoint
g(\zeta)\mapsto(\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\,g)({\bm{x}},s)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}:L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})\to L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),

(7.3)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\,g)({\bm{x}},s):=\varphi_{\mathbf{I}}({\bm{x}},s)^{\top}\,g\big({\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\big),\quad({\bm{x}},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}.

(7.4)

Further, define the unitary scaling operators on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,g(\zeta):=\delta^{-1/2}\,g(\delta^{-1}\,\zeta)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,g(\zeta)=\delta^{1/2}\,g(\delta\,\zeta),\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{R}.

(7.5)

and introduce the composition:

\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}:=\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})).

(7.6)

The results of Section
4.4
may be summarized as follows: for any given
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta)
, an eigenpair
(z_{j}(\widehat{\mu}),\,\alpha^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}_{j}(\cdot\,;\,\widehat{\mu}))\in\mathbb{R}\times L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
of the effective Dirac operator
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\widehat{\mu})
gives rise, for any
\mu
in compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}
, to a
\mathcal{O}(\delta)
–approximate eigenvalue
E_{D}+\delta\mskip 1.5muz_{j}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})
of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu}
, with an associated
\mathcal{O}(\delta)
–approximate eigenfunction, defined by (
4.18
), expressible as

\Psi^{\delta,(0)}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{I}},j}=\delta^{-1/2}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}^{*}\,\alpha^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}_{j}(\cdot\,;\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}).

(7.7)

This expression underlies the role played by
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}
in our main Theorem
7.1
, below.

By arranging the families
\smash{(\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}})_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}}
and
\smash{(\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}})_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}}
into columns, we obtain

\mathcal{T}:=\begin{pmatrix}\vdots\\
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\\
\vdots\end{pmatrix},\quad\mathcal{J}_{\delta}:=\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}=\begin{pmatrix}\vdots\\
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\\
\vdots\end{pmatrix}:L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})\to[L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})]^{\mathbb{L}}.

(7.8)

Proposition
9.1
presents some useful properties for
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
,
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}
,
\mathcal{T}
,
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}
. In particular, we prove that the column operators
\mathcal{T},\mathcal{J}_{\delta}
are bounded:

\forall\;q\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad\mathcal{T},\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}(H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),\,\ell^{2}_{q}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})).

(7.9)

Here,
H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
is defined by (
5.4
), and for any Banach space
\mathscr{X}
,
\ell^{2}_{q}(\mathbb{L};\mathscr{X})
is defined by

\ell^{2}_{q}(\mathbb{L};\mathscr{X}):=\bigg\{(g_{\mathbf{I}})_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}\in\mathscr{X}^{\mathbb{L}}\ \ \Big|\ \ \sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}(1+m^{2}_{\mathbf{I}})^{q}\,\|g_{\mathbf{I}}\|^{2}_{\mathscr{X}}<\infty\bigg\},

(7.10)

and is equipped with the norm arising from (
7.10
).
We use the following abbreviated notations:
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};\mathscr{X})=\ell^{2}_{q=0}(\mathbb{L};\mathscr{X})
,
\ell^{2}_{q}(\mathbb{L})=\ell^{2}_{q}(\mathbb{L};\mathbb{C})
, and
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L})=\ell^{2}_{q=0}(\mathbb{L};\mathbb{C})
.

In preparation for our resolvent expansion, we introduce the block-diagonal Dirac operator defined for
(\mu,\delta)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{*}_{+}
by

\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)=\begin{pmatrix}\ddots&&(0)\\
&\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})&\\
(0)&&\ddots\end{pmatrix}_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}:\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))\to\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})),\quad\textnormal{that is},\\[8.0pt]
\forall\;\bm{g}=(g_{\mathbf{I}})_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})),\quad\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)\,\bm{g}\big)_{\mathbf{I}}:=\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})\,g_{\mathbf{I}},\quad\forall\;{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}.

(7.11)

The operators
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})
are the effective Dirac operators, arising in our formal multiple scale expansion; see (
4.14
).

For any
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}
, we introduce a block-diagonal restriction operator, denoted
\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
. For every
\bm{g}=(g_{\mathbf{I}})_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
, we define
\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;\bm{g}\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
by

\big(\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;\bm{g}\big)_{\mathbf{I}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l@{\quad}l}g_{\mathbf{I}},&\forall\;{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\\[8.0pt]
0,&\forall\;{\mathbf{I}}\not\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}.\end{array}\right.

(7.12)

Clearly,
\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
is bounded on
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
.

Our main result states that after an energy-centering about
E=E_{D}
and
\delta
–dependent rescaling of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}
,
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu
, so that the bulk energy gap is of order one, the resolvent is well-approximated, for
\delta\to 0
, by the resolvent of the block-diagonal effective Dirac operator
\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)
.

Theorem 7.1

(Resolvent expansion for general edges)
.

Let
{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}={\mathbf{v}}_{1}+r{\mathbf{v}}_{2}
, where
r\in\mathbb{R}
. Suppose that the conditions (

⁢
H
.1

)–(

⁢
H
.3

) hold. Fix
\eta,\mu_{0}>0
, and a compact set
S\subset\mathbb{C}
. There exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that if

\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\quad\mu\in[-\mu_{0},\mu_{0}],\quad k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu,\quad z\in S,\quad\operatorname{dist}\big(z,\;\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)\big)>\eta,

then the operator
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}-\delta\,z
is invertible from
L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
H^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
, and we have the expansion:

\bigg(\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}}{\delta}-z\mskip 1.5mu\bigg)^{-1}=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\\
+\frac{1}{\eta}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4}),\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{k_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\parallel$}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})).

(7.13)

Remark 7.2
.

By simply replacing
{\mathbf{K}}
with
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}
in the various objects involved in the statement of Theorem
7.1
, a similar resolvent expansion can be established for parallel quasi-momenta located in a neighborhood of
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
.

Theorem
7.1
is a result on the centered and
\delta
–scaled operator
\delta^{-1}\,(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu}-E_{D})
. The resolvent of this operator is shown in (
7.13
) to be similar, at first-order, to the resolvent of the block-diagonal Dirac operator
\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)
defined by (
7.11
). For rational edges, a similar expansion was derived in
[
43
]
for the two-dimensional Schrödinger operator
-\nabla\cdot A^{\delta}({\bm{x}})\,\nabla+V({\bm{x}})
acting on an appropriate space. Here, working with the augmented Schrödinger operator enables us to study irrational edges. In Section
A.2
, we discuss this expansion for rational edges.

Given its definition (
7.13
), the spectral properties of
\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)
can be derived from those of the effective Dirac operators
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
defined in (
4.14
) for
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
,
\widehat{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}
. For instance,

\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)=\bigcup_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}\,\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}).

As stated in Proposition
4.6
and shown in Figure
3
, the essential spectrum of each effective operator
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\widehat{\mu})
is purely absolutely continuous, and is given by the complement in
\mathbb{R}
of some interval
(-\theta_{\textnormal{gap}}(\widehat{\mu}),\theta_{\textnormal{gap}}(\widehat{\mu}))
containing finitely many simple and isolated eigenvalues. If
r
is rational, then
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}
vanishes for
\delta
small enough and
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
(see Proposition
A.1
), so that
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})=\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\mu)
. In that case, the spectrum of
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)
contains
finitely
many eigenvalues of
infinite
multiplicity. However, if
r
is irrational, then the family
(\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}
is dense in
[-\pi,\pi]
. As a consequence, the spectrum of
\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)
is
\mathbb{R}
, and contains a
dense
set of
simple
eigenvalues, a subset of which is dense in the bulk gap
(-\theta_{\textnormal{gap}},\theta_{\textnormal{gap}})
, and a subset of which is embedded in its absolutely continuous part
\mathbb{R}\setminus(-\theta_{\textnormal{gap}},\theta_{\textnormal{gap}})
. This is illustrated in Figure
7
. In particular, for
r\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathbb{Q}
, the assumption on
z
in Theorem
7.1
reduces to
|\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits z|>\eta
.

Figure 7
.
The spectrum of the block-diagonal operator
\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)
, obtained by sampling the spectrum of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\widehat{\mu})
at
\widehat{\mu}=\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}
for
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}
. The red dots are the eigenvalues embedded in the gap
(-\theta_{\textnormal{gap}},\theta_{\textnormal{gap}})
. The blue dots represent the eigenvalues embedded in the absolutely continuous spectrum of
\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)
, and extend to
\theta^{\delta}_{\textnormal{gap}}:=\theta_{\textnormal{gap}}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\mskip 1.5mu\pi)
. For rational edges,
\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)
has a finite number of eigenvalues of
infinite
multiplicity (top panel), whereas for irrational edges,
\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)
has a
dense
set of simple eigenvalues (bottom panel).

Remark 7.3
.

For technical reasons, the quantity
\delta^{3/4}
appearing in the resolvent expansion cannot be replaced by
\delta
. More generally, one can replace
\delta^{3/4}
by
\delta^{\nu}
, which changes the order of the error term in (
7.13
). The exponent
\nu
has to be taken in a subinterval of
(0,1)
, which is determined in the proof of Theorem
7.1
. For rational edges,
\nu\in(1/2,1)
, and
\nu=2/3
, as chosen in
[
43
]
, yields a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/3})
–error term. For irrational edges,
\nu\in(2/3,1)
, and based on our calculations, the optimal (though likely non-sharp) error order is
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
, achieved when
\nu=3/4
. The difference in outcomes is due to the existence of constants
C_{0},C_{1}>0
such that

\forall\;{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{\nu}),\ \ \|\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})\|_{\mathscr{L}(H^{1}(\mathbb{R}),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))}\underset{\delta\to 0}{\sim}C_{0}+C_{1}\,|\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}|.

For rational edges,
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}
vanishes for a small enough
\delta
and for
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{\nu})
(Proposition
A.1
), so that the
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
norm of the Dirac operator is bounded in
\delta
. However, if
r
is irrational, then since
(\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{\nu})}
is dense in
[-\delta^{\nu-1},\delta^{\nu-1}]
, and
\nu<1
, the above norm is allowed to tend to infinity as
\delta\to 0
. The treatment of this unboundedness property is what leads to a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
–error, which is larger than the
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/3})
–error of the rational case.

8.
First part of the proof: Schur complement reduction

We first conjugate
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu}
to an operator
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)
which acts, for each
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
, on the fixed space
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle=\big(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}+\mathrm{i}\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\cdot A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}+\mathrm{i}\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\big)+V_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s).

(8.1)

The proof of Theorem
7.1
uses the overall strategy in
[
46
,
43
]
:

(
1
).

Projections on the modes near and away from the Dirac point:

The multiple scale analysis of Section
4
produces wave packet type states, which are spectrally concentrated in a neighborhood of the Dirac point of
\mathcal{H}^{0}=-\Delta+V
. This motivates, in Section
8.1
, our introducing projectors:
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
and
\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}={\operatorname{Id}}-\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
, to spectrally localize elements of
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
near and away from the Dirac point. The construction of
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
relies heavily on two ingredients:
(i)
Proposition
5.3
, which shows that elements of
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
can be decomposed in terms of Floquet-Bloch modes with quasi-momenta located on the line
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
, and
(ii)
Section
6.3
, where we characterized the values of
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
for which
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
lies in a neighborhood of the lattice of all high-symmetry quasi-moments:
\mathbb{K}=({\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*})\cup({\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*})
.

(
2
).

Schur complement / Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction scheme:

Writing
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})=\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}\oplus\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}
where
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}:=\Ran(\Pi_{\mathrm{near}})
and
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}:=\Ran(\Pi_{\mathrm{far}})
, the operator
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z
can be expressed in block form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{A}&\mathbf{B}\\
\mathbf{C}&\mathbf{D}\end{pmatrix},

where
\mathbf{A}:\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}\to\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}
,
\mathbf{B}:\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}\to\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}
,
\mathbf{C}:\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}\to\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}
, and
\mathbf{D}:\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}\to\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}
. In Section
8.2
, we prove that
\mathbf{D}=\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}
is invertible under the omnidirectional no-fold condition (

⁢
H
.3

); see also Definition
6.2
. Further, we prove that the off-diagonal operators
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{C}
have norms that tend to zero, as
\delta\to 0
, and hence the invertibility of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z
reduces to that of
\mathbf{A}=\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
.

(
3
).

Asymptotic expansion of the resolvent for
\delta\downarrow 0
:

In Section
9
, we expand the inverse of
\mathbf{A}
. In Section
9.1
, we prove some useful properties of the operators
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
,
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}
,
\mathcal{T}
,
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}
, and their adjoints. Section
9.2
expands the near projector
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
in terms of the operators
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}
involved in Theorem
7.1
; see Proposition
8.2
. Then, in Section
9.3
, we show that
\mathbf{A}
is asymptotically governed by a family of band-limited Dirac operators; see Proposition
8.6
. At last, Section
9.4
contains the expansion of
(\mathbf{A}/\delta)^{-1}
(Proposition
8.7
) in terms of
(\mathcal{D}^{\delta}(\mu)-z)^{-1}
, which we use to conclude the proof of Theorem
7.1
.

8.1.
Projectors near and far from Dirac points

Let
({\mathbf{K}}_{\star},E_{D})
, with
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
, denote a Dirac point in the sense of Section
2.5
. We recall from Section
2.2
that for any
{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathscr{B}
, the
L^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}
–spectrum of
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\mathbf{k}}=-\Delta+V({\bm{x}})
consists of eigenvalues
E_{b}({\mathbf{k}})
, with associated eigenfunctions
\Phi_{b}(\cdot\,;{\mathbf{k}})
, for
b\geq 1
. By (

⁢
H
.1

), there exists an integer
b_{\star}\geq 1
such that, setting
\{-,+\}:=\{b_{\star},b_{\star}+1\}
, the energy
E_{D}=E_{+}({\mathbf{K}}_{\star})=E_{-}({\mathbf{K}}_{\star})
is a double eigenvalue of
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}
, for
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
. For arbitrary
{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathscr{B}
, we introduce the orthogonal projector
\Pi^{0}_{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{\mathbf{k}})
:

\forall\;u\in L^{2}_{\mathbf{k}},\quad\Pi^{0}_{\mathbf{k}}\,u({\bm{x}})=\sum_{\pm}\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\Phi_{\pm}(\cdot\,;{\mathbf{k}}),\,u\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}}\,\Phi_{\pm}({\bm{x}};{\mathbf{k}}),\quad{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

(8.2)

For
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
,
\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{k}}={\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}
can be rewritten in terms of the Dirac eigenbasis
\{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{1},\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{2}\}
:

\forall\;u\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}},\quad\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\mskip 1.5muu({\bm{x}})=\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}},\,u\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}},\quad{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

(8.3)

Recall, by Proposition
5.3
, that any
F\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
can be represented as a continuous superposition of Floquet-Bloch modes of
\mathcal{H}^{0}=-\Delta+V({\bm{x}})
with quasi-momenta located on the line
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
. For
\delta>0
, Proposition
6.4
ensures that
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
belongs to the
\delta^{3/4}
–neighborhood,
B

δ

3
/
4

of
\mathbb{K}
, the lattice of high-symmetry quasi-momenta, if and only if
{\lambda}\in{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+(-{\delta^{3/4}},{\delta^{3/4}})
for some
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
. Here,
{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{R}
is defined by (
6.11
), and the set
\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})\subset\mathbb{L}
is introduced in (
6.22
). Using these observations, we introduce, for any
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
, the orthogonal projector on
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
, associated with the two dispersion surfaces touching at the Dirac point. In (
5.17b
), replacing the infinite sum over
b\geq 1
by a finite sum over
b\in\{b_{\star},b_{\star}+1\}
and inserting a Fourier-truncation to the interval
|{\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq\delta^{3/4}
, we define
for
F\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
:

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}\,F({\bm{x}},s)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi\big(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\big)\,\sum_{\pm}\widetilde{F}_{\pm}({\lambda})\,\Phi_{\pm}({\bm{x}};{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,d{\lambda},

(8.4a)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi\big(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\big)\,\big[\mskip 1.5mu\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}\,\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\big]({\bm{x}})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,d{\lambda},

(8.4b)

where
\widetilde{F}_{-}({\lambda}):=\widetilde{F}_{b_{\star}}({\lambda})
,
\widetilde{F}_{+}({\lambda}):=\widetilde{F}_{b_{\star}+1}({\lambda})
. Here,
\chi
denotes the characteristic function of the interval
[-1,1]
. The second equality follows from the definition (
5.17c
) of
\widetilde{F}_{b}({\lambda})
and the definition (
8.2
) of
\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}
. Note that the projection
\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}
only involves the Floquet-Bloch eigenfunctions
\Phi_{\pm}({\bm{x}};{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})
. The corresponding components are then integrated over the quasi-momentum segment
\{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2},\ |{\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq\delta^{3/4}\}
, which is included in
B_{\delta^{3/4}}
, provided that
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
. Using the definition (
5.14
) of
\mathscr{F}^{*}_{\mathbf{K}}
, we find the equivalent definition:

\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}\,F:=\mathscr{F}^{*}_{\mathbf{K}}\Bigl\{\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\mapsto\chi\big(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\big)\,\big[\mskip 1.5mu\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}\,\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\big]({\bm{x}})\mskip 1.5mu\Bigr\}.

(8.4c)

We also introduce the following operators:

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\Pi_{\mathbf{I}},\quad\textrm{where}\quad\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4}),\quad

(8.5a)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}

\displaystyle:=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}=\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\Pi_{\mathbf{I}},\quad\text{and}\quad

(8.5b)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}

\displaystyle:={\operatorname{Id}}_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}-\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}.

(8.5c)

Following Proposition
6.4
, we choose
\delta
so that

0<\delta^{3/4}<1/3;\quad\textnormal{hence ${\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+(-\delta^{3/4},\,\delta^{3/4})$ are pairwise disjoint for ${\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})$.}

(8.6)

Under Condition (
8.6
),

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{J}}=0,\quad\textrm{for}\quad{\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}},\quad

(8.7a)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}=0,\quad\text{and}\quad

(8.7b)

\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
,
\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}
and
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
for
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
, define orthogonal projectors on
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
.

(8.7c)

As a consequence, their ranges

\mathscr{X}_{\mathbf{I}}:=\Ran(\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}),\quad\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}:=\Ran(\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}),\quad\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{near}}}:=\Ran(\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}),\quad\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{far}}}:=\Ran(\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}),

(8.8)

are all closed subspaces of
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
.

Proposition 8.1
.

For
q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and for any
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
, the orthogonal projector
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
is bounded from
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
.

Proof
.

Let
F\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
and
q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. By definition (
8.4a
) of
\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}
,

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,F({\bm{x}},s)=\sum_{\pm}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\chi\big(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\big)\Big]\,\widetilde{F}_{\pm}({\lambda})\,\Phi_{\pm}({\bm{x}};{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,d{\lambda}.

Applying the characterization (
5.18
), we then get

\|\mskip 1.5mu\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,F\mskip 1.5mu\|^{2}_{H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}\underset{\eqref{eq:link_augmented_Sobolev_regularity_decay_FB_coeffs}}{\hyperref@@ii[item:notation_lesssim_simeq]{\simeq}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{b\in\{b_{\star},b_{\star}+1\}}(1+b^{2})^{q/2}\,\Big[\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\chi\big(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\big)\Big]^{2}|\widetilde{F}_{b}({\lambda})|^{2}\,d{\lambda}\\
\lesssim C_{q}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\chi\big(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\big)\Big]^{2}\,\sum_{\pm}|\widetilde{F}_{\pm}({\lambda})|^{2}\,d{\lambda},\quad\text{where}\quad C_{q}:=\bigl[\mskip 1.5mu1+(b_{\star}+1)^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\bigr]^{q/2}.

Since
\chi
is the characteristic function of
[-1,1]
and the intervals
{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}+(-\delta^{3/4},\delta^{3/4})
,
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
, are pairwise disjoint by (
8.6
), we have

\forall\;\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4}),\quad\Big\|\mskip 1.5mu\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\chi\big(\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-3/4}(\cdot-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\mskip 1.5mu\big)\mskip 1.5mu\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}=1.

(8.9)

As a consequence,

\|\mskip 1.5mu\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,F\mskip 1.5mu\|^{2}_{H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}\lesssim C_{q}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{\pm}|\widetilde{F}_{\pm}({\lambda})|^{2}\,d{\lambda}\lesssim C_{q}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}}=C_{q}\,\|\mskip 1.5muF\mskip 1.5mu\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})},

where the last equality follows from the Plancherel-like formula (
5.13
). The above estimate shows that
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
is indeed bounded from
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
.

Even though the individual eigenmode maps
{\mathbf{k}}\mapsto\Phi_{\pm}({\bm{x}};{\mathbf{k}})
are discontinuous in a neighborhood of
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}
(see
[
63
, Theorem 3.2]
or
[
61
, Corollary 3.3]
), the projection-valued map
{\mathbf{k}}\mapsto\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\Pi^{0}_{\mathbf{k}}\;\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}
is smooth, and can be expanded using Taylor series
[
98
]
. Using this observation, in Section
9.2
(see Part
(b)
of Proposition
9.2
), we prove the following result, on the asymptotic behavior of the near quasi-momentum projectors.

Proposition 8.2
.

As
\delta\to 0
, uniformly in subsets
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
,

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})).

(8.10)

Here,
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}
and
\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}
are defined by (
7.1
)–(
7.8
),
\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
denotes the block-diagonal restriction operator on
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
defined by (
7.12
), and
\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta}):=\mathcal{F}^{*}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,\cdot)\,\mathcal{F}
(see (
1.6
)), where
\mathcal{F}
denotes the Fourier transform on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

8.2.
A Schur complement reduction to near spectral components

The projectors
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
and
\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}
allow us to decompose elements of
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
in terms of “near” components and “far” components:

\displaystyle L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})=\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{near}}}\oplus\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{far}}},\quad\text{where}\quad\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{near}}}:=\Ran(\Pi_{\mathrm{near}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{far}}}:=\Ran(\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}).

(Recall that the projections
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
and
\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}
, and thus the corresponding subspaces
\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{near}}}
and
\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{far}}}
are
\delta
-dependent.) Accordingly,
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z
, for
z\in\mathbb{C}
, admits the following block representation, as an operator acting on
\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{near}}}\oplus\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{far}}}
:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\ &\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\\[8.0pt]
\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\ &\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\end{pmatrix}.

For
\delta
small, we shall implement a reduction to
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}
based on the Schur complement.

Lemma 8.3

(Schur complement)
.

Let
\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}
denote Banach spaces. Consider the block operator matrix

\mathcal{M}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{A}&\mathbf{B}\\
\mathbf{C}&\mathbf{D}\end{pmatrix}:\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathcal{M})=\mathscr{X}\oplus\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D})\subset\mathscr{X}\oplus\mathscr{Y}\to\mathscr{X}\oplus\mathscr{Y},

where
\mathbf{A}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X})
and
\mathbf{C}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})
are bounded, and
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{D}
are possibly unbounded with dense domains
\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D})\subseteq\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{B})\subseteq\mathscr{Y}
. Suppose that
\mathbf{D}
is invertible on
\mathscr{Y}
with bounded inverse
\mathbf{D}^{-1}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{Y},\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D}))
. Introduce the Schur complement
\mathbf{S}:=\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\mathbf{C}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X})
.
Finally, suppose
\mathbf{S}
is invertible on
\mathscr{X}
.

Then,
\mathcal{M}
is invertible on
\mathscr{X}\oplus\mathscr{Y}
, and its inverse,
\mathcal{M}^{-1}
, is bounded from
\mathscr{X}\oplus\mathscr{Y}
to
\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathcal{M})=\mathscr{X}\oplus\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D})
and is explicitly given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{S}^{-1}&-\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\\[8.0pt]
-\mathbf{D}^{-1}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{S}^{-1}&\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{D}^{-1}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\ .

To study the resolvent of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)
near the Dirac energy,
E_{D}
, we shall apply Lemma
8.3
with Banach spaces
\mathscr{X}=\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}
,
\mathscr{Y}=\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}
, and block operators:

\begin{array}[]{l@{\qquad}l}\mathbf{A}:=\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}},&\mathbf{B}:=\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}},\\[12.0pt]
\mathbf{C}:=\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}},&\mathbf{D}:=\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}.\end{array}

By Proposition
8.1
, we have that
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
is bounded from
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
H^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
, and it follows that
\mathbf{A}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}})
. On the other hand,
\mathbf{D}
is unbounded, with domain

\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D}):=H^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})\cap\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}.

(8.11)

Moreover, note from its definition (
8.1
) that

\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)=\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-2\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\mu\mskip 1.5mu\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}+(\mu\mskip 1.5mu\delta)^{2}\,|{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}|^{2}\\[4.0pt]
+\delta\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\mu\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\cdot\Big[\mskip 1.5mua({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\kappa\big(\delta\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\big)\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\mu\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\big).

(8.12)

From (
5.19
) and the definition (
8.4b
) of
\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}
, it follows that
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}
commutes with each projector
\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}
, and therefore with
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
. Since
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}=0
, we deduce that

\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}=\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}=0.

Therefore, (
8.12
) combined with the boundedness of
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
from
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
H^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
implies that

\mathbf{C}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}},\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{C}=\mathcal{O}(\delta)\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}},\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}).

(8.13a)

Taking the adjoint bound yields

\mathbf{B}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}},\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{B}=\mathcal{O}(\delta)\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}},\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}).

(8.13b)

The implicit constants in the bounds (
8.13
) are uniform in
(\mu,z)
varying in compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}
.

To apply Lemma
