8.3
, we first need to verify that
\mathbf{D}
is boundedly invertible from
\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{far}}}
to its domain
\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D})
defined by (
8.11
) and equipped with the norm on
H^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
. This is precisely where the omnidirectional no-fold condition, (
6.4
), becomes relevant. Under this assumption, the
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
–band dispersion slices
{\lambda}\mapsto E_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})
are bounded away from the Dirac energy
E_{D}
whenever
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
lies outside a neighborhood of
\mathbb{K}:=({\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*})\cup({\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*})
, the lattice of high-symmetry quasi-momenta:

Lemma 8.4
.

Assume Hypothesis (

⁢
H
.3

), i.e. that the omnidirectional no-fold condition (
6.4
) holds. Then, there exist constants
\delta_{0},C_{0},C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\delta_{0}<\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{k}},\mathbb{K})\ \

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\ \ |E_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}})-E_{D}|\geq C_{0}

(8.14a)

\displaystyle\delta^{3/4}<\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{k}},\mathbb{K})\leq\delta_{0}\ \

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\ \ |E_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}})-E_{D}|\geq C_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{3/4},

(8.14b)

\displaystyle b\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{b_{\star},b_{\star}+1\}\ \

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\ \ |E_{b}({\mathbf{k}})-E_{D}|\geq C_{2}\,(1+b),\quad{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

(8.14c)

Proof
.

Estimate (
8.14a
).
We proceed by contradiction: assume that for
\delta_{0}>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
{\mathbf{k}}_{n}\in\mathscr{B}
such that
\delta_{0}<\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{k}}_{n},\mathbb{K})
and
|E_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}}_{n})-E_{D}|\leq 1/n
. Since the sequence
({\mathbf{k}}_{n})_{n}
is bounded, one can extract a subsequence converging to some
\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}}\in\mathscr{B}
as
n\to+\infty
. It follows that
0<\delta_{0}\leq\operatorname{dist}(\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}},\mathbb{K})
, whereas, by continuity of
{\mathbf{k}}\mapsto E_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}})
, we have
E_{\pm}(\widehat{{\mathbf{k}}})=E_{D}
. But Condition (
6.4
) requires that
\hat{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{K}
, which contradicts that
\hat{\mathbf{k}}
is bounded away from
\mathbb{K}
.

Estimate (
8.14b
).
The conical behavior (
2.7
) of the dispersion surfaces
E_{\pm}
around the Dirac point implies the existence of constants
\delta_{1},C>0
, as well as functions
e_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}})
defined for
\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{k}},\mathbb{K})<\delta_{1}
such that

|e_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}})|\leq C\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{k}},\mathbb{K}),\quad E_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}})-E_{D}=\pm\upsilon_{D}\,\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{k}},\mathbb{K})\,(1+e_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}})).

(8.15)

Since
\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{k}},\mathbb{K})<\delta_{1}\Longrightarrow 1+e_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}})\geq 1-C\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{k}},\mathbb{K})
, we choose
\delta_{0}:=\min(\delta_{1},1/C)/2
so that
\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{k}},\mathbb{K})\leq\delta_{0}<\delta_{1}\Longrightarrow 1+e_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}})>1-C\delta_{0}=:\widetilde{C}>0
. For such values of
{\mathbf{k}}
, we deduce directly from (
8.15
) that
\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{k}},\mathbb{K})\in(\delta^{3/4},\delta_{0}]\Longrightarrow|E_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}})-E_{D}|\geq\upsilon_{D}\,\operatorname{dist}({\mathbf{k}},\mathbb{K})\,\widetilde{C}\geq C_{1}\delta^{3/4}
, where
C_{1}:=\upsilon_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{C}>0
.

Estimate (
8.14c
).
The first step of the proof of
[
46
, Lemma 4.1]
implies that if the no-fold condition (
6.4
) holds, then
\pm(E_{\pm}({\mathbf{k}})-E_{D})\geq 0
for
{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Hence, (
8.14c
) follows from the fact that the dispersion functions are labeled in increasing order, and from Weyl’s law; see also
[
61
, Equation (7.21)]
.

The invertibility of
\mathbf{D}
then follows from the behavior of the dispersion surfaces far from the Dirac point:

Proposition 8.5
.

Assume (

⁢
H
.3

), that is, the omnidirectional no-fold condition (
6.4
). Consider
\mu_{0}>0
and a compact set
S\subset\mathbb{C}
. There exists a constant
\delta_{0}>0
depending on
\mu_{0},S
such that for
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,
|\mu|\leq\mu_{0}
,
z\in S
, the operator

\mathbf{D}=\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}

is invertible from
\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{far}}}
to its domain
\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D})
defined by (
8.11
). In addition, equipping
\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D})
with the
H^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
–norm, we have
\mathbf{D}^{-1}=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{-3/4})
in
\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{far}}},\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D}))
, uniformly in
z\in S
and
\mu\in[-\mu_{0},\mu_{0}]
.

Proof
.

Let
(\mu,z)\in[-\mu_{0},\mu_{0}]\times S
. By (
8.12
), uniformly in
(\mu,z)
varying in compact subsets,

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}=\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta)\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D}),\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}).

Therefore, it suffices to first prove that
\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}
is invertible from
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}
to
\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D})
with a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{-3/4})
–bounded inverse, and then conclude to the invertibility of
\mathbf{D}
using a Neumann series argument.

By completeness of the Floquet-Bloch modes
\{\Phi_{b}(\cdot\,;{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\}_{b\geq 1,{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}}
in
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
(see Proposition
5.3
), and by definition (
8.4a
,
8.5
) of
\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}
, any
F\in\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}
is given by

F({\bm{x}},s)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi^{\delta}_{\mathrm{far}}({\lambda})\,\sum_{\pm}\widetilde{F}_{\pm}({\lambda})\,\Phi_{\pm}({\bm{x}};{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,d{\lambda}\\[-4.0pt]
+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{b_{\star},b_{\star}+1\}}\widetilde{F}_{b}({\lambda})\,\Phi_{b}({\bm{x}};{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,d{\lambda},\quad({\bm{x}},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}.

Here,
\widetilde{F}_{-}({\lambda}):=\widetilde{F}_{b_{\star}}({\lambda})
,
\widetilde{F}_{+}({\lambda}):=\widetilde{F}_{b_{\star}+1}({\lambda})
, where
\widetilde{F}_{b}({\lambda})
is defined by (
5.17c
), and

\chi^{\delta}_{\mathrm{far}}({\lambda}):=1-\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\chi\big(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\big).

We introduce the function in
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}
, defined by

U({\bm{x}},s)=\sum_{b\geq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{U}_{b}({\lambda})\,\Phi_{b}({\bm{x}};{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,d{\lambda},\quad\text{for}\quad({\bm{x}},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R},

with
\widetilde{U}_{-}({\lambda})=\widetilde{U}_{b_{\star}}({\lambda})
,
\widetilde{U}_{+}({\lambda})=\widetilde{U}_{b_{\star}+1}({\lambda})
, and where

\widetilde{U}_{b}({\lambda}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\textFOR}l}\displaystyle\chi^{\delta}_{\mathrm{far}}({\lambda})\,\big(\mskip 1.5muE_{-}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\widetilde{F}_{-}({\lambda})\quad\text{for}\quad&b=b_{\star},\\[8.0pt]
\displaystyle\chi^{\delta}_{\mathrm{far}}({\lambda})\,\big(\mskip 1.5muE_{+}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\widetilde{F}_{+}({\lambda})\quad\text{for}\quad&b=b_{\star}+1,\\[8.0pt]
\displaystyle\big(\mskip 1.5muE_{b}({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\widetilde{F}_{b}({\lambda})\quad\text{for}\quad&b\not\in\{b_{\star},b_{\star}+1\}.\end{array}\right.

It follows from Lemma
8.4
that for
\delta
small enough,

|\widetilde{U}_{\pm}({\lambda})|\underset{(\ref{eq:dispersion_curves_bounded_away_from_Dirac_1},\ref{eq:dispersion_curves_bounded_away_from_Dirac_2})}{\lesssim}\delta^{-3/4}\,|\widetilde{F}_{\pm}({\lambda})|\quad\text{and}\quad(1+b)\,|\widetilde{U}_{b}({\lambda})|\underset{\eqref{eq:dispersion_curves_bounded_away_from_Dirac_3}}{\lesssim}|\widetilde{F}_{b}({\lambda})|\quad\forall\;b\neq\pm,

where the implied constants are independent of
{\lambda}
. These estimates, squared and summed over
b\geq 1
,
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
, lead to

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{b\geq 1}(1+b)^{2}\,|\widetilde{U}_{b}({\lambda})|^{2}\lesssim\delta^{-3/4}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{b\geq 1}|\widetilde{F}_{b}({\lambda})|^{2},

or equivalently, using the characterization (
5.18
):

\|\mskip 1.5muU\mskip 1.5mu\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}\lesssim\delta^{-3/4}\,\|\mskip 1.5muF\mskip 1.5mu\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}.

(8.16)

In particular,
U\in H^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
, and from the spectral representation (
5.20
) of
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}
, we see that

\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,U=F.

(8.17)

Equations (
8.16
,
8.17
) imply that
\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{far}}
is boundedly invertible from
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}
to
H^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})\cap\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{far}}=\textnormal{D\kern 0.83344pt}(\mathbf{D})
with a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{-3/4})
inverse.

Now that the invertibility of
\mathbf{D}
has been established, it remains to study the invertibility of the Schur complement
\mathbf{S}:=\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\mathbf{C}
on
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}
. Using Proposition
8.5
and (
8.13
), one has

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1-3/4+1})

\displaystyle\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}})

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{5/4})

\displaystyle\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}),

(8.18)

uniformly in
(\mu,z)
varying in compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}
. In particular, it suffices to study the invertibility of
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
, and then pass to
\mathbf{S}
using a Neumann series argument. This is the object of the next results.

Recall the block-diagonal effective Dirac operator,
\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)
(see (
7.11
)), emerging from the multiple scale asymptotic analysis of Section
4
.

Proposition 8.6
.

Consider
\mu_{0}>0
and a compact set
S\subset\mathbb{C}
. There exists
\delta_{0}>0
, depending on
\mu_{0}
and
S
, such that for all
0<\delta<\delta_{0}
, the following operator expansions hold, with error terms which are uniform in
\mu\in[-\mu_{0},\mu_{0}]
,
z\in S
and
\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
:

(a).

The asymptotic behavior of
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
is governed by a band-limited block-diagonal effective Dirac operator. More precisely,

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
=\delta\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)-z\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/2})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}).

We recall that
\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\Ran(\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}})
and
\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta}):=\mathcal{F}^{*}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,\cdot)\,\mathcal{F}
; see also (
1.6
).

(b).

If
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}
are disjoint, then

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{7/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mskip 1.5mu\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}},\,\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mskip 1.5mu).

Proposition
8.6
describes the asymptotic behavior of
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
. Its inverse is expanded in the next result.

Proposition 8.7
.

Fix
\mu_{0},\eta>0
and a compact set
S\subset\mathbb{C}
. Suppose that

\exists\;\delta_{0},C>0,\quad\forall\;\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\quad\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4}),\quad\mu\in[-\mu_{0},\mu_{0}],\quad z\in S,\\[4.0pt]
\big\|\mskip 1.5mu\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\;\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mskip 1.5mu\big\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mskip 1.5muL^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))\mskip 1.5mu)}\leq C/\eta.

(8.19)

Then
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
is invertible on
\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
, and its inverse admits the following expansion:

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\bigg(\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}}{\delta}-z\mskip 1.5mu\bigg)^{-1}\!\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\\
=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\;\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{\eta}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mskip 1.5mu\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mskip 1.5mu).

(8.20)

Remark 8.8
.

(a).

The condition
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
\operatorname{dist}(z,\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu))>\eta
implies (
8.19
). In fact, if
\operatorname{dist}(z,\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu))>\eta
, then the resolvent
(\mathcal{D}^{\delta}(\mu)-z)^{-1}
is bounded in
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
with a norm less than
1/\eta
. Therefore,
\mathcal{D}^{\delta}(\mu)-z
is invertible from from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
. Further, by the closed graph theorem, its inverse is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
. Combining this with the boundedness (
7.9
) of
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}
, we deduce (
8.19
) with
C:=\|\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\|=\|\mathcal{T}\|
which is independent of
\delta
.

(b).

The condition
\operatorname{dist}(z,\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu))>\eta
implies (
8.19
) (as explained in the first part of this remark) but the converse is
not
true. In fact, in subsequent work
[
4
]
, under a Diophantine condition on the edge parameter
r
, we shall prove that (
8.19
) holds even when
z
is an eigenvalue of the effective Dirac operator
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\,\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})
such that
{\mathbf{I}}\not\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
. This result will be used in the construction of augmented edge states.

Finally, we use the next result to reformulate the dominant term in (
8.20
) for
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}=\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
.

Proposition 8.9
.

Consider
\eta,\mu_{0}>0
, a compact set
S\subset\mathbb{C}
, and assume that there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that

\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\quad\mu\in[-\mu_{0},\mu_{0}],\quad k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu,\quad z\in S,\quad\operatorname{dist}\big(z,\;\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)\big)>\eta,

as in Theorem
7.1
. Then, as
\delta\to 0
,

\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\\
=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;\mathcal{J}_{\delta}+\frac{1}{\eta}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})).

In other words, the near projectors can be removed from the left-hand side, at the cost of introducing a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
–error.

Proposition
8.6
is the analog of
[
43
, Proposition 6.4]
, while Propositions
8.7
and
8.9
together form an analog of
[
43
, Proposition 6.5]
. Their proofs are presented in Section
9
. We now show that Theorem
7.1
follows from these results.

Proof
(of Theorem
7.1
using Propositions
8.6
,
8.7
, and
8.9
).

The assumption on
z
in Theorem
7.1
implies (
8.19
); see Remark
8.8.

(
a
)

. Thus, by applying Proposition
8.7
with
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}=\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
, we obtain that
\Pi_{{\mathrm{near}}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{{\mathrm{near}}}
is invertible on
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}
. Using a Neumann series argument, we deduce from (
8.18
), for
\delta
sufficiently small, that
\mathbf{S}
is invertible on
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}}
and the following holds in
\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}})
:

\displaystyle(\mathbf{S}/\delta)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\mskip 1.5mu\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\bigg(\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}}{\delta}-z\mskip 1.5mu\bigg)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}})}(\delta^{1/4})\mskip 1.5mu\bigg]^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\bigg(\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}}{\delta}-z\mskip 1.5mu\bigg)^{-1}\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}+\frac{1}{\eta}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4}).

(8.21)

The last equality follows from the expansion (
8.20
).
In particular,
\mathbf{S}^{-1}=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{-1})
in
\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}})
. Moreover, since by (
8.13
) and Proposition
8.5
,

\mathbf{C}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}},\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{far}}})}(\delta),\quad\mathbf{B}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{H}^{2}_{{\mathrm{far}}},\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{near}})}(\delta),\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{D}_{1}^{-1}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{far}}},\mathscr{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{far}})}(\delta^{-3/4}),

Lemma
8.3
implies the following in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
:

(\mathcal{M}/\delta)^{-1}=\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}}{\delta}-z\bigg)^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}(\mathbf{S}/\delta)^{-1}&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4}).

(8.22)

Substituting (
8.21
) in (
8.22
), and using Proposition
8.9
to remove the projectors
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}
, we obtain in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
the following:

\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)-E_{D}}{\delta}-z\bigg)^{-1}\!\!=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}+\frac{1}{\eta}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4}).

By combining this expansion with the definition (
8.12
) of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)
in terms of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu}
, we deduce the resolvent expansion for general edges, Theorem
7.1
.

9.
Second part of the proof: Asymptotic expansions and estimates

In this technical section, we carefully derive the asymptotic expansions and estimates stated in Propositions
8.2
,
8.6
,
8.7
, and
8.9
. We follow the strategy of
[
43
, Section 6]
. To simplify the presentation, we set

\mu=0,\quad\textnormal{so that}\quad\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)=\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}.

The forthcoming calculations extend to any
\mu
varying in a compact subset of
\mathbb{R}
.

In Section
9.1
, we present some properties of the operators
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}
. Section
9.2
contains the proof of Proposition
8.2
on the asymptotic behavior of near projectors, as well as part of the proof of Proposition
8.6
on the asymptotic behavior of the operator
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}
projected on near components. The proof of Proposition
8.6
is concluded in Section
9.3
. Finally, in Section
9.4
, we prove the resolvent estimates in Propositions
8.7
and
8.9
, therefore completing the proof of Theorem
7.1
.

9.1.
Spectral localization tools: properties of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}

We refer to Section
7
and Equations (
7.1
)–(
7.6
) for the definitions of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
,
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}
,
\mathcal{T}
,
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}
, and their adjoints. In this section, we summarize some useful properties of these operators, via the following proposition. Recall that the Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}:g(\zeta)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})\mapsto\mathcal{F}g({\lambda})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
is given by (
1.5
), and the partial Fourier transform
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}:F({\bm{x}},s)\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})\mapsto\widetilde{F}({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})
is defined by (
5.9
).

Proposition 9.1

(Properties of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}
)
.

Let
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}},m_{\mathbf{I}})\in\mathbb{L}=\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}\times\mathbb{Z}
and further let
{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}
and
{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda}):=\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
be as in the Wrapping Lemma
6.3
.

(a).

Expression for
\mathcal{F}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
: Let
{\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}\in[-\pi,\pi)
. Then, for all
F\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),

(\mathcal{F}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}})\,F\,({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})=\left\langle\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\,{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}}),\;\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}{F}({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\right\rangle_{L_{\bm{x}}^{2}(\Omega)}

(9.1)

(b).

Expression for
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}^{*}\mathcal{F}^{*}
: For
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
and all
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
:

\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,(\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\,\mathcal{F}^{*})\,g({\bm{x}},{\lambda})=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,g({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}),\quad{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\ .

(9.2)

(c).

(Orthogonality relations)
\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}
and
\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}
are isometries:

\mathcal{T}_{{\mathbf{I}}}\,\mathcal{T}^{*}_{{\mathbf{I}}}=\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}={\operatorname{Id}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})}\ .

(9.3)

(d).

(Boundedness of
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}
) For any
q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the column operators
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}
are bounded from the space
H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
defined by (
5.4
) to the space
\ell^{2}_{q}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
defined by (
7.10
), uniformly in
\delta
: in fact, there exists a constant
C_{q}>0
depending on the
\mathscr{C}^{q}
–norm of
(\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}},\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}})^{\top}
such that

\forall\;F\in H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),\quad\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}(1+m^{2}_{\mathbf{I}})^{q}\;\|\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\mskip 1.5mu\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{q}\,\|F\|^{2}_{H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}.

(9.4)

Proof
.

(a)
and
(b)
. Relations satisfied by
\mathcal{F}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
and its adjoint
. It suffices to establish the expression (
9.2
); the relation (
9.1
) follows by a duality argument.
To prove (
9.2
), we replace
g(\cdot)
by
\mathcal{F}^{*}g(\cdot)
in the definition of
\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}
, and use (
7.1
), which defines
\varphi_{\mathbf{I}}({\bm{x}},s)
in terms of
\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}=(\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}_{1},\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}_{2})
:

(\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\;\mathcal{F}^{*}\,g)({\bm{x}},s)=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\scalebox{0.7}{$\big($}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}+({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}+{\mathbf{K}})\cdot s\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\scalebox{0.7}{$\big)$}}\ \Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\ (\mathcal{F}^{*}g)\big({\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\big).

Applying the partial Fourier transform
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
, (
5.9
), yields

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,(\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\,\mathcal{F}^{*}\,g)({\bm{x}},{\lambda})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\;\mathcal{F}^{*}\,g)({\bm{x}},s)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,ds

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F}^{*}\,g)\mskip 1.5mu\big({\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\big)\ \mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}+{\mathbf{K}})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\ \mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}\,ds

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F}^{*}\,g)\mskip 1.5mu(s+{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{x}})\ \mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\mskip 1.5mus}\,ds

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu(\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}){\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\ \Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{F}^{*}\,g)\mskip 1.5mu(t)\ \mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\mskip 1.5mut}\,dt\ =\ \mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\ \Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\ g({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\ .

In the last equality we used relation (
6.10
):
{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})=\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\in\widehat{\Omega}
of the Wrapping Lemma
6.3
. This completes the proof of (
9.2
).

(c)
. Orthogonality relations
. (
9.3
) follows from the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
and its adjoint, and from the fact that
\{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}_{1},\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}_{2}\}
is orthonormal; see Condition

(

⁢
i
i

)

in Proposition
2.2
.

(d)
. Bound (
9.4
)
. Let
F\in H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
. Fix
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
, and let
{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\}\times\mathbb{Z}
. By substituting (
6.9
),
i.e.
the relation
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}
, in the relation (
9.1
), we find

\mathcal{F}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\,({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})=\widehat{F}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\lambda}),\quad\text{where}\quad{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}\in\Lambda^{*}\quad\text{and}\quad\\[8.0pt]
\widehat{F}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{{\mathbf{k}}}({\lambda}):=\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\mathbf{k}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\left[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})}\mskip 1.5mu\right]\left[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\,({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\right]\,d{\bm{x}},\quad\forall\;{\mathbf{k}}\in\Lambda^{*}.

(9.5)

Since
\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}
is
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}
-pseudo-periodic and
{\bm{x}}\mapsto\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F({\bm{x}},{\lambda})
is
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
-pseudo-periodic with respect to
\Lambda
, the bracketed terms in the integrand are
\Lambda
– periodic functions of
{\bm{x}}
.
Hence, for
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\widehat{F}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{{\mathbf{k}}}({\lambda})
is the Fourier coefficient with index
{\mathbf{k}}
of a
\Lambda
–periodic function.
Moreover, from its definition (
6.13
), the family
({\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}})_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\}\times\mathbb{Z}}
is pairwise disjoint in
\Lambda^{*}
. Therefore, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
F
, such that

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\}\times\mathbb{Z}}(1+m^{2}_{\mathbf{I}})^{q}\;\|\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\,\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\}\times\mathbb{Z}}(1+m^{2}_{\mathbf{I}})^{q}\;\|\mathcal{F}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\,\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

by Plancherel’s theorem

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\}\times\mathbb{Z}}(1+m^{2}_{\mathbf{I}})^{q}\;\|\widehat{F}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

by (
9.5
)

\displaystyle\leq C\,\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\}\times\mathbb{Z}}(1+|{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}|^{2})^{q}\;\|\widehat{F}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

because
{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2}=m_{\mathbf{I}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}\in\Lambda^{*}}(1+|{\mathbf{k}}|^{2})^{q}\;\|\widehat{F}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{\mathbf{k}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=C\,\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}\in\Lambda^{*}}(1+|{\mathbf{k}}|^{2})^{q}\;|\widehat{F}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}_{{\mathbf{k}}}({\lambda})|^{2}\,d{\lambda},

where the last equality is obtained by interchanging the sum over
{\mathbf{k}}\in\Lambda^{*}
and the integral over
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
, via Tonelli’s theorem. Now, note that
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\in H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}
, since
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
is isomorphic from
H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})
, according to Proposition
5.2
. Therefore, using the characterization (
2.2
) of elements of
H^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{2}/\Lambda)
in terms of their Fourier series, and the fact that
\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we deduce the estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\}\times\mathbb{Z}}(1+m^{2}_{\mathbf{I}})^{q}\;

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\,\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq:link_Sobolev_regularity_decay_Fourier_coeffs}}{\lesssim}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\|{\bm{x}}\mapsto\left[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})}\mskip 1.5mu\right]\left[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\,({\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\right]\right\|^{2}_{H^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{2}/\Lambda)}\,d{\lambda}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\|^{2}_{\mathscr{C}^{q}}\;\|\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}F\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})}\ \lesssim\ \|\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\|^{2}_{\mathscr{C}^{q}}\;\|F\|^{2}_{H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}.

For the last bound, we reused the boundendess of
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}
from
H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\lambda};H^{q}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})
. Summing this bound over
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
leads to (
9.4
), which proves the boundedness of
\mathcal{T}
. Moreover since
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}=\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{U}_{\delta}
is bounded uniformly in
\delta
, it follows that
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}
is bounded uniformly in
\delta
.

9.2.
Proof of Proposition
8.2
and expansion of the unperturbed operator
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}
on near components

In this section, we study the asymptotic behavior of the unperturbed operator
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}
, projected on near components. More precisely, in Proposition
9.2
, we derive an expansion for
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu)^{d}\;\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}
, where
d=0,1
, and
\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}
are subsets of
\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
. The motivation behind this expansion is two-fold: first, for
d=0
, it provides an expansion for the near projectors, hence proving Proposition
8.2
. Second, for
d=1
, it captures the effective Dirac Hamiltonian character of the unperturbed operator
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}
, when spectrally localized near the Dirac (conical) point. The latter result will be used in the subsequent proof of Proposition
8.6
in Section
9.3
.

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
d\in\{0,1\}
.

(a).

Assume
\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
and
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}=\emptyset
. Then,
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu)^{d}\;\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}=0.

(b).

As
\delta\to 0
, the following holds in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
, uniformly in
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
:

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\;\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
=\delta^{d}\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\big[\mskip 1.5mu\sigma({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,D_{\zeta}+\delta^{-1}\,\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,\sigma({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})\mskip 1.5mu\big]^{d}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}+\mathcal{O}\big(\delta^{\frac{3}{4}(d+1)}\big),

(9.6)

where
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}
is given by (
7.6
), and
\sigma({\mathfrak{K}}_{j})
is the
2\times 2
Hermitian matrix defined in (
4.3
). In particular, for
d=0
, (
9.6
) reduces to the expansion of
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
, displayed in (
8.10
) of Proposition
8.2
.

Proof
.

Part
(a)
of Proposition
9.2
derives from the fact that
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}
commutes with
\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}
, and
\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}\,\Pi_{\mathbf{J}}=0
for
{\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}}
; see (
8.7
). This also implies that

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\;\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\;\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}.

(9.7)

We now turn to the proof of Part
(b)
of Proposition
9.2
. We first show that the operator
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;(\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D})^{d}\;\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
is approximately equal to a sum of operators:
\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}
, where each
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}
is given explicitly in terms of a Dirac eigenspace projection. The latter expression is then shown, for
\delta
small, to be equal to the dominant term in (
9.6
).

Step 1.

Expansion near the Dirac point
. Fix
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}
. From the integral representation (
5.19
) of
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}
in terms of
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}
, and from the definition (
8.4c
) of
\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\;\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}\,F

\displaystyle=\mathscr{F}^{*}_{\mathbf{K}}\,\biggl\{\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\mapsto\chi\big(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\big)\,\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\;\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}\,\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\Big]({\bm{x}})\mskip 1.5mu\biggr\}.

To expand this operator, we use the following asymptotic expansion for
d=0,1
, shown for instance in
[
43
, Equation (
6.9
) (
d=0
) and Step
2
of Proposition
6.6
(
d=1
)]
. For any
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
, as
|{\bm{\ell}}|\to 0
, there holds in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}})
:

\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}}\;(\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}}-E_{D})^{d}\;\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}+{\bm{\ell}}}=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\bm{\ell}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\;(\mskip 1.5mu-2\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}\cdot\nabla\mskip 1.5mu)^{d}\;\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\,{\bm{\ell}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}+\mathcal{O}(|{\bm{\ell}}|^{d+1}).

(9.8)

Let
{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
. By Part

(
d
)

of the Wrapping Lemma
6.3
, we have
{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}
, where
{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}\in\Lambda^{*}
. Therefore, using the
\Lambda^{*}
–periodicity of
{\mathbf{k}}\mapsto(\Pi^{0}_{\mathbf{k}},\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\mathbf{k}})
, we have

\Pi^{0}_{\smash{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\vphantom{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})}}}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\smash{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\vphantom{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})}}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\;\Pi^{0}_{\smash{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\vphantom{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})}}}=\Pi^{0}_{\smash{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})}}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\smash{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\;\Pi^{0}_{\smash{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})}}.

(9.9)

Now, assume
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
and
|{\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq\delta^{3/4}
, so that
{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})=\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4})
. Then, applying the expansion (
9.8
) with
{\bm{\ell}}:={\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})
leads to the following in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}})
:

\displaystyle\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\;\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})+\mathcal{O}\big(\delta^{\frac{3}{4}(d+1)}\big),\quad|{\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}|\leq\delta^{3/4},\ {\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4}),

\displaystyle\quad\text{with}\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda}):=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu-2\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\;\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\,{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot{\bm{x}}},

(9.10)

and where the implicit constant in the error term can be chosen to be independent of
{\mathbf{I}}
. Introducing the operator
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}
defined for
F\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
by

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F:=\mathscr{F}^{*}_{\mathbf{K}}\,\Bigl\{\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{x}},{\lambda})\mapsto\chi\big(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\big)\,\big[\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\,\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\big]({\bm{x}})\mskip 1.5mu\Bigr\},

(9.11)

it follows from the Plancherel-like formula (
5.13
) and the estimates (
8.9
,
9.10
) that

\displaystyle\Big\|\mskip 1.5mu\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\;\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}-\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\mskip 1.5muF\,\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq:directional_Plancherel}}{=}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big|\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\!\chi\big(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\big)\Big|^{2}\,\Big\|\mskip 1.5mu\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\,\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!d{\lambda}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:sum_indicators_bounded},\ref{eq:asymptotics_Pi0k_p_lvark2_H_E_Pi0k_p_lvark2})}{\lesssim}\delta^{\frac{3}{2}(d+1)}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}+{\lambda}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}}}\,d{\lambda}\underset{\eqref{eq:directional_Plancherel}}{=}\delta^{\frac{3}{2}(d+1)}\;\|\mskip 1.5muF\mskip 1.5mu\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}.

Combining this bound with (
9.7
), we deduce the following expansion in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
, uniformly in
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
:

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{d}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}+\mathcal{O}\big(\delta^{\frac{3}{4}(d+1)}\big).

(9.12)

It remains to prove that
\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}
coincides with the first expression on the right-hand side of (
9.6
). This is the object of the next step.

Step 2.

Reformulation of the dominant operator
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}
. Let
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
be arbitrary. In this step, we prove the identity:

\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbf{I}}}\,F=\delta^{d}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\;\big[\mskip 1.5mu\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,D_{\zeta}+\delta^{-1}\,\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})\mskip 1.5mu\big]^{d}\;\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,F.

(9.13)

Then Part
(b)
of Proposition
9.2
will follow from summing (
9.13
) over
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
, and using the expansion (
9.12
) of Step 1.

Let
F\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
and recall that
\widetilde{F}:=\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,F
(see (
5.9
)). We begin by reformulating the function
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\,\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})
defined by (
9.10
), using the alternative expression (
8.3
) for
\Pi^{0}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}
:

\displaystyle\big[\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\,\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\big]({\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\big\langle\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}},(-2\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot\nabla)^{d}\;\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}}\,\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot{\bm{y}}}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{y}}),\,\widetilde{F}({\bm{y}},{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\bm{y}}(\Omega)}.

Recall that
{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})=\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}+({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
. The product
\langle\mskip 1.5mu\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}},(-2\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot\nabla)^{d}\;\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\mskip 1.5mu\rangle\in\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2}
is the identity matrix when
d=0
, by orthonormality of
\{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}_{1},\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}_{2}\}
. For
d=1
, it equals the matrix
\sigma({\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda}))=({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})+\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})
defined by (
4.3
), by Proposition
4.3
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\big[\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\,\widetilde{F}(\cdot\,,{\lambda})\big]({\bm{x}})

(9.14)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\big[\mskip 1.5mu({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})+\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})\mskip 1.5mu\big]^{d}\,\big\langle\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot{\bm{y}}}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{y}}),\,\widetilde{F}({\bm{y}},{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\bm{y}}(\Omega)}.

Multiplying each side of (
9.14
) by
\chi(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}))
yields an expression for
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F({\bm{x}},{\lambda})
, where
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}
is defined by (
9.11
). Further, using the formulas (
9.1
,
9.2
) satisfied by
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
, (
7.2
), and its adjoint, (
7.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F({\bm{x}},{\lambda})

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq:averaging_operator_alt}}{=}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\bm{\ell}}_{\mathbf{I}}({\lambda})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\big[\mskip 1.5mu({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})+\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})\mskip 1.5mu\big]^{d}\,\chi\big(\delta^{-3/4}({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\big)\,\mathcal{F}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\,({\lambda}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq:averaging_operator_alt_adjoint}}{=}\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\,\mathcal{F}^{*}\Big\{\mskip 1.5mu\widehat{{\lambda}}\mapsto\big[\mskip 1.5mu\widehat{{\lambda}}\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})+\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})\mskip 1.5mu\big]^{d}\,\chi(\delta^{-3/4}\,\widehat{{\lambda}})\mskip 1.5mu\Big\}\,\mathcal{F}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\,({\bm{x}},{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\,\big[\mskip 1.5mu\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,D_{\zeta}+\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})\mskip 1.5mu\big]^{d}\,\chi(\delta^{-3/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\,({\bm{x}},{\lambda}).

Using the relation
f(D_{\zeta})=\mathcal{U}_{\delta}^{*}\,f(\delta\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}
with
f({\lambda}):=[\mskip 1.5mu\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,{\lambda}+\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})\mskip 1.5mu]^{d}\,\chi(\delta^{-3/4}\,{\lambda})
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F({\bm{x}},{\lambda})=\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\,\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\big[\mskip 1.5mu\delta\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,D_{\zeta}+\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})\mskip 1.5mu\big]^{d}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\,({\bm{x}},{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\delta^{d}\,\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{K}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\big[\mskip 1.5mu\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,D_{\zeta}+\delta^{-1}\,\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,\sigma^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})\mskip 1.5mu\big]^{d}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,F\,({\bm{x}},{\lambda}),

where the last equality follows from the definition of
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}:=\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
. Finally, applying
\mathscr{F}^{*}_{\mathbf{K}}
leads to (
9.13
).

9.3.
Proof of Proposition
8.6
: expansion of the domain wall contribution

In Section
9.3.1
, we study the contributions from the domain wall perturbation, when projected on near components; see Proposition
9.3
. In Section
9.3.2
, we will combine this with results from Section
9.2
, and complete the proof of Proposition
8.6
.

9.3.1.
Expansion of
\ \Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}
; contribution from the domain wall

Define

\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s):=\kappa\big(\delta\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\big).

The main result of this section is the following.

Proposition 9.3
.

As
\delta\to 0
, the following expansion holds in
\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}},\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}})
, uniformly in subsets
\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
:

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
=\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\,\sigma_{3}\,\kappa(\zeta)\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4}).

(9.15)

The main difficulty in proving Proposition
9.3
is that, unlike
\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}
, the domain wall term
\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}
does
not
commute with
\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}
. As a result, it couples elements lying in the ranges of distinct projectors
\Pi_{\mathbf{I}}
and
\Pi_{\mathbf{J}}
for
{\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}}
. We address this issue in two steps, starting with the following lemma.

Lemma 9.4
.

As
\delta\to 0
, the following holds in
\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}},\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}})
, uniformly
\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
:

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
=\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\,\zeta}\,\kappa(\zeta)\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4}).

(9.16)

Here
M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\in\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2}
is defined for any
{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
as

M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}:=\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,({\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}-{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{J}})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})}\,\nabla\cdot a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,d{\bm{x}},

(9.17)

where
{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}\in\Lambda^{*}
in introduced in the Wrapping Lemma
6.3
; see (
6.13
).

Proof
.

By Proposition
8.2
,
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4})
in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
, uniformly in
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
. By writing the same expansion for
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}
, and by using the boundedness properties of
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}},\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}
(Proposition
8.1
), we deduce the following asymptotic expansion in
\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}},\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}})
, uniformly in
\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
:

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{A}^{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4}).

(9.18)

Here,
\mathcal{A}^{\delta}\in\mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})))
is the block operator whose entries are given by

\mathcal{A}^{\delta}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\displaystyle\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta}),&({\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}})\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime},\\[12.0pt]
0&({\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}})\not\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}.\end{array}\right.

(9.19)

In what follows, we decompose
\mathcal{A}^{\delta}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}
, with the goal of extracting the dominant term in (
9.16
).

Step 1.

Preliminary decomposition of
\mathcal{A}^{\delta}
. We introduce the notations: for
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
and
{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
,

\displaystyle a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle:=(\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}}))_{j,l=1,2},

\displaystyle\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)

\displaystyle:=\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}+({\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}}+{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\mskip 1.5mus\ \ \textnormal{so that}\ \ \varphi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)\underset{\eqref{eq:mode_for_averaging_operator}}{=}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\;\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}}),

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{\delta}_{n,{\mathbf{J}}}(D_{\zeta})

\displaystyle:=\delta\,\Upsilon^{\delta}_{\mathbf{J}}(0;D_{\zeta})\cdot\bm{e}_{n}:=\mathrm{i}\left(\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}{\mathfrak{K}}^{(n)}_{1}+\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}^{(n)}_{2}D_{\zeta}\right),\quad n=1,2,

(9.20)

\displaystyle g_{\delta}(\zeta)

\displaystyle:=\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,g(\zeta).

Here,
\Upsilon^{\delta}_{\mathbf{J}}(\mu;D_{\zeta})
is the operator introduced in (
4.8
), and
\bm{e}_{1}:=(1,0)^{\top}
,
\bm{e}_{2}:=(0,1)^{\top}
. Note that
g_{\delta}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, since
\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,\cdot)
is compactly supported. We also require the following identity for future reference: for
u({\bm{x}},\zeta)\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3};\mathbb{C}^{2})
,
f(\zeta)\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
, and
l^{\prime}=1,2
, by the product rule,

U({\bm{x}},s)=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\;u({\bm{x}},s)^{\top}\,f(\zeta)\big|_{\zeta=\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\\[8.0pt]
\begin{aligned} \Longrightarrow\ \ \partial_{x_{l^{\prime}}}U({\bm{x}},s)&=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\;\partial_{x_{l^{\prime}}}u({\bm{x}},s)^{\top}f(\zeta)\big|_{\zeta=\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\\
&+\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\;u({\bm{x}},s)^{\top}\left(\mskip 1.5mu\Upsilon^{\delta}_{l^{\prime},{\mathbf{J}}}(D_{\zeta})\,f\mskip 1.5mu\right)\mskip 1.5mu(\zeta)\big|_{\zeta=\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}.\end{aligned}

(9.21)

Let
({\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}})\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}
,
j,l\in\{1,2\}
and
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
. With the above notations, it follows from the definition
\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}:=\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{J}}\,\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}
and from the definition (
7.1
,
7.4
) of
\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{J}}
that

\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}\,g_{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{x}},s)=\delta^{1/2}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\;\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,g_{\delta}(\zeta)\big|_{\zeta=\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}.

Differentiating the above expression with respect to
x_{l}
, and applying the product rule (
9.21
) with
u({\bm{x}},s)=\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})
,
f=g_{\delta}
,
l^{\prime}=l
, we obtain:

\partial_{x_{l}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}\,g_{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{x}},s)=\delta^{1/2}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\;\partial_{x_{l}}\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}g_{\delta}(\zeta)\big|_{\zeta=\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\\[4.0pt]
+\delta^{1/2}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\;\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\left(\mskip 1.5mu\Upsilon^{\delta}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}(D_{\zeta})\,g_{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\right)(\zeta)\big|_{\zeta=\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}.

(9.22)

Multiplying (
9.22
) by
\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)
yields an expression with two terms on the right-hand side. The first term is of the form
U({\bm{x}},s)
in (
9.21
) with
u({\bm{x}},s)=\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\partial_{x_{l}}\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})
and
f=g_{\delta}
. The second term is also of the form
U({\bm{x}},s)
with
u({\bm{x}},s)=\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})
and
f=\Upsilon^{\delta}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}(D_{\zeta})\,g_{\delta}
. Therefore, we can differentiate these two terms with respect to
x_{j}
and use the product rule (
9.21
) with
l^{\prime}=j
to obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\partial_{x_{j}}\left(\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\partial_{x_{l}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}\,g_{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\right)({\bm{x}},s)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &=\left.\delta^{1/2}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\;\partial_{x_{j}}\left(\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\partial_{x_{l}}\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\mskip 1.5mu\right)\times g_{\delta}(\zeta)\right|_{\zeta=\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\\[4.0pt]
&+\;\delta^{1/2}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\left(\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\partial_{x_{l}}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\mskip 1.5mu\right)\times\left.\left(\mskip 1.5mu\Upsilon^{\delta}_{j,{\mathbf{J}}}(D_{\zeta})\,g_{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\right)(\zeta)\right|_{\zeta=\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\\[4.0pt]
&+\;\delta^{1/2}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\partial_{x_{j}}\left(\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\mskip 1.5mu\right)\times\left.\left(\mskip 1.5mu\Upsilon^{\delta}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}(D_{\zeta})\,g_{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\right)(\zeta)\right|_{\zeta=\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\\[4.0pt]
&+\;\delta^{1/2}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\left(\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\mskip 1.5mu\right)\times\left.\left(\mskip 1.5mu\Upsilon^{\delta}_{j,{\mathbf{J}}}(D_{\zeta})\,\Upsilon^{\delta}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}(D_{\zeta})\,g_{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\right)(\zeta)\right|_{\zeta=\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\end{aligned}

(9.23)

\displaystyle:=G^{(0)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}},s)+G^{(1)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}},s)+G^{(2)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}},s)+G^{(3)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}},s),

(9.24)

where
G^{(n)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}},s)\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
,
n=0,\dots,3
, are the four terms on the right-hand side of (
9.23
). Now, for
n\in\{0,\dots,3\}
, let
\mathcal{A}^{\delta,(n)}
be the block operator acting on
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
, whose entries are given for any
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
by

\mathcal{A}^{\delta,(n)}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\,g:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\displaystyle\sum_{j,l=1}^{2}\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,G^{(n)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}),&({\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}})\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime},\\[8.0pt]
0,&({\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}})\not\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}.\end{array}\right.

(9.25)

Then summing (
9.24
) over
j,l=1,2
, applying
\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}
, and using
g_{\delta}=\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,g
yields an expression for the entries of
\mathcal{A}^{\delta}
, (
9.19
): for
({\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}})\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}
,

\mathcal{A}^{\delta}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\underset{\eqref{eq:def_A_I_J_dem_DW}}{=}\sum_{j,l=1}^{2}\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\partial_{x_{l}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\underset{(\ref{eq:dem_DW_2bis},\ref{eq:def_A_n_I_J_delta_dem_DW})}{=}\sum_{n=0}^{3}\mathcal{A}^{\delta,(n)}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}.

(9.26)

We claim (and prove in the next steps below) that

\displaystyle\forall\;({\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}})\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime},\quad\mathcal{A}^{\delta,(0)}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}

\displaystyle=\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\,\zeta}\,\kappa(\zeta)\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta}),

(9.27)

\displaystyle\forall\;n\in\{1,\dots,3\},\quad\mathcal{A}^{\delta,(n)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4}),\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})),

(9.28)

uniformly in
\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
, where
M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\in\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2}
is defined by (
9.17
). Note that if these two statements hold, then together with (
9.26
) and (
9.18
) they directly imply the desired result, Lemma
9.4
. It therefore remains to establish (
9.27
,
9.28
). We do so in the next two steps.

Step 2.

Proof of Identity (
9.27
)
. Let
({\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}})\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}
. From the definitions (
7.1
)–(
7.6
), it follows that for
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,G^{(0)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}(\zeta)=\delta^{-1/2}\,\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{I}}\scalebox{0.82}{$($}{\bm{x}},\,\delta^{-1}\,\zeta-{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{x}}\scalebox{0.82}{$)$}}\;\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})}\,G^{(0)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}},\delta^{-1}\,\zeta-{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{x}})\,d{\bm{x}}.

Then substituting
G^{(0)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}
with its definition (
9.23
), and using
\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)=\kappa(\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}))
as well as the identity
\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)-\xi_{\mathbf{I}}({\bm{x}},s)=(\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}-\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}+({\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,s
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{j,l=1}^{2}\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,G^{(0)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}(\zeta)=M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\,\zeta}\,\kappa(\zeta)\,\left(\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,g\mskip 1.5mu\right)(\zeta),\quad\text{where}\quad

\displaystyle M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}:=\sum_{j,l=1}^{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\scalebox{0.82}{$($}(\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}-\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}-({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}}){\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\scalebox{0.82}{$)$}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\,\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\partial_{x_{l}}\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,d{\bm{x}}.

Finally, using the relation
(\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}-\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}-({\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}-{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{J}}
, which follows from Part

(
d
)

the Wrapping Lemma
6.3
, as well as the definition
a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}=(\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}}))_{j,l=1,2}
, we deduce that the above definition of
M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}
coincides with (
9.17
). Substituting this in the definition (
9.25
), we then obtain (
9.27
).

Step 3.

Proof of Bound (
9.28
)
. We only present the arguments for
n=1
, since they extend straightforwardly to
n=2,3
. Let
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
and
{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
. By (
9.23
),

G^{(1)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}},s):=\delta^{1/2}\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\xi_{\mathbf{J}}({\bm{x}},s)}\mskip 1.5mu\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\mskip 1.5mu\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\partial_{x_{l}}\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\left(\mskip 1.5mu\Upsilon^{\delta}_{j,{\mathbf{I}}}(D_{\zeta})\,g_{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\right)(\zeta)\big|_{\zeta=\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}.

(9.29)

To reformulate this definition, introduce the operator
\mathcal{S}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}\in\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
whose adjoint is defined by

\mathcal{S}^{*}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}\in\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}),L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})),\quad\mathcal{S}^{*}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}\,g({\bm{x}},s):=\partial_{x_{l}}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}g(\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})).

Note that
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}
is defined similarly to
\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{J}}
, (
7.4
), where
\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}
is replaced by
\partial_{x_{l}}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}
. The same holds for
\mathcal{S}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}
in terms of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{J}}
. The definitions of
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}
,
\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}
, and
g_{\delta}=\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,g
, substituted in Equation (
9.29
), lead to

G^{(1)}_{j,l,\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}},s):=\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\mathcal{S}^{*}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}\;\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,\Upsilon^{\delta}_{j,{\mathbf{I}}}(D_{\zeta})\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,g\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{x}},s).

(9.30)

Summing (
9.30
) over
j,l=1,2
and applying
\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}
yields an expression for
\mathcal{A}^{\delta,(1)}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}
, the operator defined by (
9.25
). This expression can be written in the block operator form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\delta,(1)}=\sum_{j,l=1}^{2}\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\widetilde{a}_{j,l}({\bm{x}})\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\mathcal{S}^{*}_{l}\;\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,\Upsilon^{\delta}_{j}(D_{\zeta})\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}},

(9.31)

\displaystyle\textnormal{where}\quad\mathcal{S}_{l}:=\begin{pmatrix}\vdots\\
\mathcal{S}_{l,{\mathbf{J}}}\\
\vdots\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\Upsilon^{\delta}_{j}(D_{\zeta}):=\begin{pmatrix}\ddots&&(0)\\
&\Upsilon^{\delta}_{j,{\mathbf{I}}}(D_{\zeta})&\\
(0)&&\ddots\end{pmatrix}_{{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}}.

Recall that
\mathcal{T}
is bounded from
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
, uniformly in
\delta
, by Proposition
9.1.

(
d
)

. Moreover, note that the proof of this result only relies on the smoothness of
\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}
, and therefore can be adapted to show a similar result for
\mathcal{S}_{l}
. As a consequence,
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{l}
is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
. Finally, since
\Upsilon^{\delta}_{j}(D_{\zeta})
is block-diagonal with entries given by
\smash{\mathrm{i}\,(\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}{\mathfrak{K}}^{(l)}_{1}+\delta{\mathfrak{K}}^{(l)}_{2}D_{\zeta})}
, and
\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
is, by definition (
6.22
), the set of indices
{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
such that
|\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}|\leq\delta^{3/4}
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
only, such that

\displaystyle\big\|\mskip 1.5mu\Upsilon^{\delta}_{j}(D_{\zeta})\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}\mskip 1.5mu\big\|_{\mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R})))}

\displaystyle=\sup_{{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}\big\|\mskip 1.5mu\big(\mskip 1.5mu\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}^{(j)}+\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}^{(j)}\,D_{\zeta}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\mskip 1.5mu\big\|_{\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))}

\displaystyle=\sup_{{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}\big\|\mskip 1.5mu{\lambda}\mapsto\big(\mskip 1.5mu\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}^{(j)}+\delta\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}^{(j)}\,{\lambda}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,{\lambda})\mskip 1.5mu\big\|_{\infty}\leq C\,\delta^{3/4}.

Therefore, since
a({\bm{x}})
and
\kappa(\zeta)
are bounded functions, we deduce from (
9.31
) that
\mathcal{A}^{\delta,(1)}
is bounded in
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
with a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4})
norm.

To prove Proposition
9.3
, we also require the following useful lemma.

Lemma 9.5
.

For
{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
, let
{\bm{x}}\mapsto u_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}/\Lambda;\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2})
be a smooth,
2\times 2
matrix-valued,
\Lambda
–periodic function. For
{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
, consider an operator
\mathcal{A}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})
, where
(\mathscr{X},\|\cdot\|_{\mathscr{X}})
and
(\mathscr{Y},\|\cdot\|_{\mathscr{Y}})
are Hilbert spaces. Suppose that

\forall\;{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L},\quad\|\mathcal{A}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})}\leq\left|\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,({\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{J}})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;u_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})\,d{\bm{x}}\,\right|,

(9.32)

where
|\cdot|
denotes the matrix
2
–norm. Then, the block operator
\mathcal{A}:=(\mathcal{A}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}})_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}}
is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};\mathscr{X})
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};\mathscr{Y})
.

Proof
.

By the following Schur-Holmgren-Young type inequality, the operator norm of
\mathcal{A}
can be controlled in terms of its supremum column sum norm and supremum row sum norm:

\|\mathcal{A}\|^{2}_{\mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};\mathscr{X}),\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};\mathscr{Y}))}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{1}\,{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty},\\[4.0pt]
\quad\text{where}\quad{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{1}=\sup_{{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}}\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}\|\mathcal{A}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})}\quad\text{and}\quad{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}=\sup_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}}\sum_{{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}}\|\mathcal{A}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})}.

Therefore, it suffices to bound
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{1}
and
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}
.
Fix
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
and let
{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
. From its definition (
6.13
), the family
({\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}})_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\}\times\mathbb{Z}}
is pairwise disjoint in
\Lambda^{*}
. Therefore, summing (
9.32
) over
{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\}\times\mathbb{Z}
and using the smoothness of
u_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star},{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}
, we get

\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\}\times\mathbb{Z}}\|\mathcal{A}_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\|_{\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})}\leq\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}\in\Lambda^{*}}\left|\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\mathbf{k}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;u_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star},{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})\,d{\bm{x}}\,\right|<\infty.

Summing this inequality over
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
, and taking the supremum over
{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
, we deduce that

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{1}\leq\sup_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star},{\mathbf{K}}_{\circ}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}}\bigg[\sum_{{\mathbf{k}}\in\Lambda^{*}}\left|\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\mathbf{k}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;u_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star},{\mathbf{K}}_{\circ}}({\bm{x}})\,d{\bm{x}}\,\right|\bigg].

By interchanging the roles of
{\mathbf{I}}
and
{\mathbf{J}}
, we deduce a similar inequality for
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}
. Combining both inequalities on
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{1}
and
