{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}
, we then obtain the boundedness of
\mathcal{A}
.

We now turn to the proof of Proposition
9.3
.

Proof
(of Proposition
9.3
).

We start from the expansion (
9.16
) established in Lemma
9.4
. The dominant contribution in (
9.16
) is a sum over
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}
, that can be decomposed into diagonal summands (
{\mathbf{I}}={\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}
) and off-diagonal summands (
{\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}}
). Then, comparing (
9.16
) with the statement of Proposition
9.3
, we see that it suffices to prove the identity

\forall\;{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L},\quad M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{I}}}=\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\,\sigma_{3},

(9.33)

as well as the following bound in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
, uniformly in
\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}\subset\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
:

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}\\
{\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\,\zeta}\,\kappa(\zeta)\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4}).

(9.34)

In other words, the asymptotic behavior of
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}
is mainly governed by the diagonal summands, given by (
9.33
), while the off-diagonal summands are negligible due to (
9.34
). The next steps are devoted to proving these statements.

Step 1.

Diagonal summands; identity (
9.33
)
. The identity (
9.33
) follows directly from the definition (
9.17
) of
M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}
and Proposition
4.4
: in fact,

\forall\;{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L},\quad M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{I}}}=\int_{\Omega}\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})}\;\nabla\cdot a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,d{\bm{x}}=\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\,\sigma_{3}.

Step 2.

Off-diagonal summands; bound (
9.34
)
. The left-hand side of (
9.34
), which we wish to bound, can be rewritten as

\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\operatorname{off}(\mathcal{A}^{\delta,(0)})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta},

where
\operatorname{off}(\mathcal{A}^{\delta,(0)})
is the block operator whose entries are given for
{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
by

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\,\zeta}\,\kappa(\zeta)\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})&\textnormal{if}\quad{\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}}\\[8.0pt]
0&\textnormal{if}\quad{\mathbf{I}}={\mathbf{J}}.\end{array}\right.

Our strategy is to first bound each entry, and then extend the bound to the block operator using Lemma
9.5
. By definition (
9.17
) of
M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}(\zeta)
, the definition of Fourier multiplier (
1.6
), and the properties of the Fourier transform:

\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\,\zeta}\,\kappa(\zeta)\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\\
=M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\,\zeta}\;\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\;\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\chi\big(\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-3/4}\,(D_{\zeta}+{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\kappa(\delta\,\zeta)\,\chi\big(\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-3/4}\,D_{\zeta}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\;\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}.

(9.35)

The operator
\chi(\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-3/4}\,(D_{\zeta}+{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\mskip 1.5mu)\,\kappa(\delta\,\zeta)\,\chi(\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-3/4}\,D_{\zeta}\mskip 1.5mu)\in\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))
arises in
[
43
, Step 3 of proof of Proposition 6.7]
. To estimate its norm, first note, by (
8.6
) that if
{\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}}
, then the intervals
(-\delta^{3/4},\delta^{3/4})
and
{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}}+(-\delta^{3/4},\delta^{3/4})
are disjoint if
\delta^{3/4}<1/3
. Therefore, we may write
\chi(\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-3/4}\,(D_{\zeta}+{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\mskip 1.5mu)=\chi(\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-3/4}\,(D_{\zeta}+{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\mskip 1.5mu)\,D_{\zeta}^{-1}\cdot D_{\zeta}
, where the first factor is a bounded operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Then, using that
\kappa
is smooth,
\kappa^{\prime}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
and the properties of the Fourier transform, we obtain that there is a constant
C>0
, such that for all
\delta
sufficiently small: if
{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
and
{\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}}
, then

\big\|\mskip 1.5mu\chi\big(\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-3/4}\,(D_{\zeta}+{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\kappa(\delta\,\zeta)\,\chi\big(\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-3/4}\,D_{\zeta}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\mskip 1.5mu\big\|_{\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))}\leq C\,\delta^{3/4}\ .

(9.36)

The constant
C>0
depends on
\|\kappa\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\|\kappa^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}
. Using (
9.36
) and the definition (
9.17
) of
M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}
to bound (
9.35
), we deduce that for all
{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
such that
{\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}}
,

\big\|\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,M_{{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}})\,\zeta}\,\kappa(\zeta)\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\mskip 1.5mu\big\|_{\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))}\\
\leq C\,\delta^{3/4}\,\left|\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,({\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}-{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{J}})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\overline{\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})}\,a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,d{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu\right|\ .

(9.37)

Since this corresponds to (
9.32
) with
u_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}:=C\,\delta^{3/4}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\mskip 1.5mu\overline{\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})}\mskip 1.5mua({\bm{x}})\mskip 1.5mu\sigma_{2}\mskip 1.5mu\nabla\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}
, we can use Lemma
9.5
, and deduce that (
9.37
) implies the bound

\|\mskip 1.5mu\operatorname{off}(\mathcal{A}^{\delta,(0)})\mskip 1.5mu\|_{\mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R})))}\lesssim\delta^{3/4}.

Finally, using the uniform boundedness of
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}
in
\delta
, we deduce (
9.34
).

9.3.2.
Completion of the proof of Proposition
8.6

Since we set
\mu=0
, we have from (
8.12
) that
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)=\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}
, and

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}-\delta z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}-\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\delta z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}+\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\delta\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot a({\bm{x}})\,\sigma_{2}\,\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}},

(9.38)

where
\kappa_{\delta}({\bm{x}},s):=\kappa(\delta\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}))
. For
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}=\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}
, we expand the first term using (
9.6
) with
d=1
, the second term using (
9.6
) with
d=0
, and the third term using (
9.15
). We then get that uniformly in
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
and in
z
varying in compact subsets:

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
=\delta\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\sigma({\mathfrak{K}}_{2})\,D_{\zeta}+\delta^{-1}\,\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}\,\sigma({\mathfrak{K}}_{1})+\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\,\sigma_{3}\,\kappa(\zeta)-z\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\\
+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/2}),\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}},\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}).

By definition (
4.13
) of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\delta^{-1}\,\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})
, this expansion is precisely Proposition
8.6.

(
a
)

. On the other hand, if
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
are disjoint, then the first part of Proposition
9.2
shows that the first two terms in (
9.38
) are zero, while the third term, according to (
9.15
), is of order
\delta\cdot\delta^{3/4}
(the dominant term in (
9.15
) is zero, since
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}=\emptyset
). We then obtain:

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{0}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}}=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4+1})=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{7/4}),\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}},\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}),

which is precisely Proposition
8.6.

(
b
)

. This completes the proof of Proposition
8.6
.

9.4.
Resolvent expansion, proof of Propositions
8.7
and
8.9

We now turn to the proof of Proposition
8.7
. As shown at the end of Section
8.2
, Theorem
7.1
then follows directly. We recall that
\mu
is set to zero for simplicity, so that
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\mu)=\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}
.

As a starting point, the following technical result provides useful bounds for the proof of the resolvent expansion.

Lemma 9.6
.

(a).

As
\delta
tends to 0,

\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}={\operatorname{Id}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta)\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})),\mskip 1.5mu\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{-1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))).

(9.39)

(b).

\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}
is asymptotically invariant under
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
: more precisely, uniformly in
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})),\,L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})).

(9.40)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),\,\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{-1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))).

(9.41)

Proof
.

(a)
.
The product
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}
. We decompose
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}
into its diagonal and off-diagonal parts. According to Proposition
9.1.

(
c
)

, the diagonal part of
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}
is the identity operator. Therefore, it suffices to prove that the off-diagonal part of
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}
is negligible. Let
{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L}
. Using the definition (
7.1
)–(
7.6
) of
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}=\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
, we see that
\smash{\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}}
is a multiplication operator: more precisely, for any
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}\;g(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\left[\mskip 1.5mu\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,\scalebox{0.675}{$\big($}\mskip 1.5mu(\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}-\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}-({\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\mskip 1.5mu\scalebox{0.675}{$\big)$}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})}\;\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,d{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu\right]\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,\zeta\vphantom{\scalebox{0.675}{$\big($}}}\,g(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\left[\mskip 1.5mu\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,({\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}-{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{J}})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})}\;\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,d{\bm{x}}\mskip 1.5mu\right]\,\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,\zeta}\,g(\zeta),

where we used the identity
(\gamma_{\mathbf{J}}-\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}-({\lambda}_{\mathbf{J}}-{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}={\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}+{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}-{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{J}}
, which follows from Part

(
d
)

of the Wrapping Lemma. Next, we prove that if
{\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}}
, then the multiplication by
\zeta\mapsto\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-1}\mskip 1.5mu({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\smash{{\mathbf{J}}}})\mskip 1.5mu\zeta}
is a bounded operator from
H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
to
H^{-1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
, with norm
\mathcal{O}(\delta)
. For any
g,h\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})
, by integration by parts,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{h(\zeta)}\cdot g(\zeta)\;\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\smash{{\mathbf{J}}}})\mskip 1.5mu\zeta}\,d\zeta\right|=\frac{\delta}{|{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\smash{{\mathbf{J}}}}|}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[\,\overline{h^{\prime}(\zeta)}\cdot g(\zeta)+\overline{h(\zeta)}\cdot g^{\prime}(\zeta)\,\big]\;\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta^{-1}\,({\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\smash{{\mathbf{J}}}})\mskip 1.5mu\zeta}\,d\zeta\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{|{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\smash{{\mathbf{J}}}}|}\,\|h\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})}\;\|g\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})}\ \leq\ C_{0}\,\delta\,\|h\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})}\;\|g\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})},

where the last inequality follows from the fact that the set
\{|{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\lambda}_{\smash{{\mathbf{J}}}}|\ /\ {\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L},\ {\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}}\}
is bounded from below by a constant
C^{-1}_{0}>0
, by definition (
6.11
) of
{\lambda}_{\mathbf{I}}
. As a consequence,

\forall\;{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{L},\quad{\mathbf{I}}\neq{\mathbf{J}},\quad\|\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{J}}}\mskip 1.5mu\|_{\mathscr{L}({H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}),\,H^{-1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})})}\\
\leq C_{0}\,\delta\,\left|\,\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,({\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{l}}_{\mathbf{J}})\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\bigl[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})}\mskip 1.5mu\bigr]\;\bigl[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\,{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\mskip 1.5mu\bigr]\,d{\bm{x}}\,\right|.

(9.42)

This inequality corresponds to (
9.32
) with
u_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}},{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}}):=[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\,{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}({\bm{x}})}\mskip 1.5mu]\,[\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\,{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\;\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{J}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\mskip 1.5mu]
. Therefore, we can apply Lemma
9.5
to deduce that the off-diagonal part of
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}
is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{-1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
with norm
\mathcal{O}(\delta)
. This proves (
9.39
).

(b)
.
Near-invariance of
\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}
under
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
. By the expansion of
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
in Proposition
8.2
, and the boundedness of
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}
uniformly in
\delta
(Proposition
9.1.

(
d
)

), we have

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})),L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})).

Using the decomposition
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}={\operatorname{Id}}+(\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}-{\operatorname{Id}})
leads to

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\\[4.0pt]
+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4})\ \ \textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})),L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})).

(9.43)

By (
9.39
),
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}-{\operatorname{Id}}
is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{-1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
with a
\mathcal{O}(\delta)
norm. Moreover,

\big\|\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\mskip 1.5mu\big\|_{\mathscr{L}(H^{-1}(\mathbb{R}),\,L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))}\leq\sup_{|{\lambda}|\leq\delta^{-1/4}}\big(1+|{\lambda}|^{2}\big)^{1/2}=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{-1/4}).

Therefore,
\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu)
is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
with a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4})
norm. Because
\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}
is bounded uniformly in
\delta
, (
9.43
) becomes

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4}),\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})),L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})).

Next, the decomposition
\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})={\operatorname{Id}}+(\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu)
leads to

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
+\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})),L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})).

(9.44)

Using the bound

\big\|\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu\big\|_{\mathscr{L}(H^{1}(\mathbb{R}),\,L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))}\leq\sup_{|{\lambda}|\geq\delta^{-1/4}}(1+|{\lambda}|^{2})^{-1/2}=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4}),

(9.45)

and the boundedness of
\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}
, we then deduce that
\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
with a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
norm. By combining this with (
9.44
), we then get (
9.40
). Moreover, (
9.41
) is simply the dual bound to (
9.40
).

Proof
(of Proposition
8.7
).

Our starting point is Proposition
8.6.

(
a
)

, which states that uniformly in
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
,

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}-\delta\,z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\delta\,\mathfrak{L}^{\delta}(z)+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/2})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}),

(9.46)

where

\mathfrak{L}^{\delta}(z):=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}.

Define

\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z):=\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}.

Step
1
is devoted to showing that
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathfrak{L}^{\delta}(z)\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
. In Step
2
, we then prove the invertibility of
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}-\delta\,z\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
and the expansion of its inverse in terms of
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
.

Step 1.

The expression
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathfrak{L}^{\delta}(z)\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
is close to
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
. One has

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathfrak{L}^{\delta}(z)\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}.

This product features
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}
, which we express as
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}={\operatorname{Id}}+(\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}-{\operatorname{Id}})
. Then

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathfrak{L}^{\delta}(z)\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\chi(\delta^{1/4}D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{1},

(9.47)

where

\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{1}:=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\mskip 1.5mu\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mskip 1.5mu\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\big)\mskip 1.5mu\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\mskip 1.5mu\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu\Big)\mskip 1.5mu\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mskip 1.5mu\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}.

We bound the
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
–norm of
\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{1}
using the triangle inequality:

\displaystyle\|\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\|_{\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\|_{\mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R})),\,L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))}

\displaystyle\times\left\|\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\right\|_{\mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R})),\,\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R})))}\;

\displaystyle\times\|\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\|_{\mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{-1}(\mathbb{R})),\,\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R})))}\,

\displaystyle\times\big\|\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}-{\operatorname{Id}}\,\big\|_{\mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R})),\,\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{-1}(\mathbb{R})))}

\displaystyle\times\big\|\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big\|_{\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),\,\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R})))}.

(9.48)

We bound each term on the right-hand side, starting from the last one. First, by assumption, the operator
\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu)^{-1}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
is bounded from
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
, with norm less than
C/\eta
. Second, by (
9.39
),
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}-{\operatorname{Id}}
is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{-1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
with a
\mathcal{O}(\delta)
norm. Third, we have the bound

\big\|\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\mskip 1.5mu\big\|_{\mathscr{L}(H^{-1}(\mathbb{R}),\,H^{1}(\mathbb{R}))}\leq\sup_{|{\lambda}|\leq\delta^{-1/4}}\big(1+|{\lambda}|^{2}\big)=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{-1/2}).

Fourth, as already discussed in Remark
7.3
, there exist constants
C_{0},C_{1}>0
such that

\left\|\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\right\|_{\mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R})),\,\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R})))}\underset{\delta\to 0}{\sim}C_{0}+C_{1}\,\sup_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}|\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}|=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{-1/4}).

Finally,
\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})
is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
uniformly in
\delta
, by Proposition
9.1.

(
d
)

. Substituting these observations in (
9.48
), we get that
\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{1}=\eta^{-1}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
, and (
Proof
) becomes:

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathfrak{L}^{\delta}(z)\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\eta^{-1}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4}).

Moreover, by substituting the identity
\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})={\operatorname{Id}}+(\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu)
, we get

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathfrak{L}^{\delta}(z)\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
+\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
+\eta^{-1}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}).

(9.49)

Call
\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{2}
the second term on the right-hand side of (
9.49
). We bound
\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{2}
by following closely
[
43
, Step 3, proof of Proposition 6.5]
. We write

\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{2}=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\big)\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\\[4.0pt]
+\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\big(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\big),\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}.

The first term vanishes because
\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,(\mskip 1.5mu\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu)=0
. In the second term, the commutator can be simplified using that the massless part of the Dirac operator
\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z
commutes with
\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})
: more precisely, calling
\operatorname{diag}((\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\,\sigma_{3})_{{\mathbf{I}}})\in\mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2})))
the block-diagonal operator whose entries are
\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\,\sigma_{3}
, the second term can be rewritten as

\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\operatorname{diag}((\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\,\sigma_{3})_{{\mathbf{I}}})\,\big[\mskip 1.5mu\kappa(\cdot),\;\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu\big]\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}.

By assumption,
\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu)^{-1}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
is bounded from
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
, with norm less than
1/\eta
. Moreover, the bound (
9.45
), combined with
\kappa\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, implies that
[\mskip 1.5mu\kappa,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})-{\operatorname{Id}}\mskip 1.5mu]=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
as an operator from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
. Finally, as
\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\operatorname{diag}((\vartheta^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}\,\sigma_{3})_{{\mathbf{I}}})
is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
uniformly in
\delta
(Proposition
9.1
), we deduce that
\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{2}=\eta^{-1}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
. The expansion (
9.49
) becomes

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathfrak{L}^{\delta}(z)\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\chi(\delta^{1/4}\,D_{\zeta})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\eta^{-1}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}).

Finally, Proposition
8.2
shows that the dominant term in this expansion is equal to
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
, plus a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3/4})
–error in
\mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}})
. Therefore,

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathfrak{L}^{\delta}(z)\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\eta^{-1}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}).

(9.50)

This concludes the first step.

Step 2.

Inverting
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}-\delta\,z\mskip 1.5mu)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
in terms of
\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
. We multiply (
9.46
) on the right with
\delta^{-1}\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
. Because
\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}
is, by assumption (
8.19
), bounded in
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
with norm less than
C/\eta
, uniformly in
z\in S
and
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
, we get that

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}-\delta\,z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;\frac{1}{\delta}\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\mathfrak{L}^{\delta}(z)\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/2})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}).

We then use the expansion (
9.50
) shown in the first step, to obtain

\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}-\delta\,z\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;\frac{1}{\delta}\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}=\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}+\eta^{-1}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}).

(9.51)

Denote by
\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{3}(z)
the
\eta^{-1}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
–error term in (
9.51
). Using a Neumann series argument, we deduce the expansion

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-E_{D}-\delta\,z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\;\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\;\big[\mskip 1.5mu{\operatorname{Id}}_{\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}}+\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{3}(z)\mskip 1.5mu\big]^{-1}\;\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\;\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\;\Bigl[\mskip 1.5mu\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)+\frac{1}{\eta}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})\mskip 1.5mu\Bigr]\;\Pi_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{L}^{\prime}}),

(9.52)

which is precisely (
8.20
). Therefore, the proof of Proposition
8.7
is complete.

We finish with the proof of Proposition
8.9
.

Proof
(of Proposition
8.9
).

Define

\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z):=\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}.

Then Proposition
8.9
is equivalent to the expansion
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}=\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)+\eta^{-1}\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
. We write

\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\;\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\;\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}=\big(\mskip 1.5mu\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\;\mathcal{J}_{\delta}^{*}\mskip 1.5mu\big)\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\;\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\mskip 1.5mu\big).

By applying (
9.40
) with
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}=\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
, we find that
\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}-\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}
is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
with a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
norm. Since
(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu)^{-1}
is by assumption bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
, uniformly in
z\in S
, with norm less than
1/\eta
, we deduce the following expansion in
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
:

\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\;\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\;\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\;\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\mskip 1.5mu\big)+\eta^{-1}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4}).

Moreover, by applying (
9.41
) with
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}=\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
, we get that
\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}-\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}
is bounded from
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{-1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
with a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
norm. Since
(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu)^{-1}
is bounded from
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};H^{-1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{L};L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
uniformly in
z\in S
, with norm less than
1/\eta
, we obtain the expansion

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}\;\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)\;\Pi_{\mathrm{near}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta}+\eta^{-1}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{R}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(z)+\eta^{-1}\,\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})),

(9.53)

which proves Proposition
8.9
.

Appendix A
Rational matters

The goal of this section is to show how the resolvent expansion in Theorem
7.1
simplifies when
r
is a rational number. Note that for rational edges, the lifting strategy adopted in this paper is not necessary, since Theorem
7.1
can be directly formulated at the level of the eigenvalue problem (
3.3
) in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
; see
[
61
,
111
,
46
,
43
]
. Thus the present section’s sole purpose is to complete the presentation.

In Section
A.1
, we recall the classification of rational edges as zigzag-type and armchair-type, and we characterize the set
\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon)
defined by (
6.22
) for
\varepsilon
small enough and for such edges. Section
A.2
shows how Theorem
7.1
simplifies for rational edges.

A.1.
Neighborhoods of high-symmetry quasi-momenta

For rational edges, it turns out that the set
\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon)\subseteq\mathbb{L}
, defined by (
6.22
), can be characterized explicitly for
\varepsilon
small enough. In fact, assume that
r=b_{1}/a_{1}
where
b_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
a_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
are coprime, with the convention that
(a_{1},b_{1})=(1,0)
if
r=0
. In
[
58
,
43
]
, rational edges are divided into two categories:

•

Zigzag-type edges
, defined by the condition
a_{1}\neq b_{1}\mod 3
, are such that the line
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
intersects the lattice
{\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*}
without being arbitrarily close to
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*}
.

•

Armchair-type edges
, defined by the condition
a_{1}=b_{1}\mod 3
, are such that
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
intersects both
{\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*}
and
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*}
.

We prove that Proposition
6.4
is consistent with this classification.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}={\mathbf{v}}_{1}+r{\mathbf{v}}_{2}
and
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}=-r{\mathbf{k}}_{1}+{\mathbf{k}}_{2}
, and suppose that
r=b_{1}/a_{1}
, where
b_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
a_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
are coprime, with the convention that
(a_{1},b_{1})=(1,0)
if
r=0
.

(a).

Zigzag-type edges: If
a_{1}\neq b_{1}\mod 3
, then for
\varepsilon>0
small enough,

\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon)=\{{\mathbf{K}}\}\times a_{1}\,\mathbb{Z}\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}=0\quad\forall\;{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon).

In other words, as
\varepsilon\to 0
, the line
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
intersects
{\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*}
, but is bounded away from
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*}
.

(b).

Armchair-type edges: If
a_{1}=b_{1}\mod 3
, then there exists an integer
m_{0}
depending on
a_{1}
and
b_{1}
, such that for
\varepsilon>0
small enough,

\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon)=\Big(\{{\mathbf{K}}\}\times a_{1}\,\mathbb{Z}\Big)\cup\Big(\{{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}\times(m_{0}+a_{1}\,\mathbb{Z})\Big),\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}=0\quad\forall\;{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon).

In other words, for
\varepsilon
small enough,
{\mathbf{K}}+\mathbb{R}\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
intersects both
{\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*}
and
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*}
.

Proof
.

Step 1.
We first consider the lattice
{\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*}
, by letting
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}},m)
, with
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. From its definition (
6.11
), we have that
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}=2\pi\operatorname{frac}\left(-mr+1/2\right)-\pi
. Therefore, the condition
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon)
, which implies that
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}
is arbitrarily small, holds if and only if
mr\in\mathbb{Z}
, that is, for integers
m\in a_{1}\,\mathbb{Z}
. Thus, for
\varepsilon>0
small enough and for both zigzag-type and armchair-type edges,
\{{\mathbf{K}}\}\times a_{1}\,\mathbb{Z}\subset\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon)
and
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}=0
for
m\in a_{1}\,\mathbb{Z}
.

Step 2.
We now focus on
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}+\Lambda^{*}
by letting
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}^{\prime},m)
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. From (
6.11
), we obtain

\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}=2\pi\mskip 1.5mu\operatorname{frac}\big(2/3-(m+2/3)\,r+1/2\big)-\pi.

Therefore, the condition
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon)
, which implies that
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}
is arbitrarily small, holds if and only if
2/3-(m+2/3)\,r
is an integer, say
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Hence, using the expression
r=b_{1}/a_{1}
, we are reduced to finding all the solutions to the equation

\textit{Find $(m,n)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}$ such that}\qquad m\mskip 1.5mub_{1}+n\mskip 1.5mua_{1}=2\,(a_{1}-b_{1})/3.

(A.1)

First, we note that this equation has a solution
only if

(a_{1}-b_{1})/3
is an integer, that is, only if
a_{1}=b_{1}\mod 3
. The contrapositive is that if
a_{1}\neq b_{1}\mod 3
, then as
\varepsilon\to 0
,
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}
lies outside a neighborhood of
0
, so
{\mathbf{I}}\not\in\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon)
for
\varepsilon
small enough. This, combined with the first part (on
{\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*}
), concludes the proof for zigzag-type edges.

Now we consider armchair-type edges, by assuming that
a_{1}=b_{1}\mod 3
. Then
c:=2\,(a_{1}-b_{1})/3\in\mathbb{Z}
. Since
a_{1}
and
b_{1}
are coprime, we have from Bézout’s theorem the existence of
(a_{2},b_{2})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
such that
a_{1}\mskip 1.5mub_{2}-a_{2}\mskip 1.5mub_{1}=1
. Moreover, any solution of (
A.1
) has the form
(m,n)=(c\mskip 1.5mub_{2}+d\mskip 1.5mua_{1},-c\mskip 1.5mua_{2}-d\mskip 1.5mub_{1})
for
d\in\mathbb{Z}
. Thus, for
\varepsilon
small enough, we deduce that
{\mathbf{I}}=({\mathbf{K}}^{\prime},m)\in\mathbb{L}(\varepsilon)
if and only if
m\in m_{0}+a_{1}\,\mathbb{Z}
with
m_{0}:=c\mskip 1.5mub_{2}
, and
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}=0
. This, combined with the first part (on
{\mathbf{K}}+\Lambda^{*}
), concludes the proof for armchair-type edges.

A.2.
The resolvent expansion for rational edges

We highlight in this section the fibered structure of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}
to illustrate how Theorem
7.1
relates to earlier works
[
40
]
on rational edges.

Assume that
r=b_{1}/a_{1}\in\mathbb{Q}
, where
b_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
a_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
are coprime, with the convention that
(a_{1},b_{1})=(1,0)
if
r=0
. Recall that
A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)
is the augmented coefficient defined by (
3.6
). Fixing
s\in\mathbb{R}
as a parameter, one sees from the periodicity properties (
3.9
) of
A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)
that
{\bm{x}}\mapsto A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)
is
\mathbb{Z}(a_{1}\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})
–periodic. Define the cylinder
\Sigma:=\mathbb{R}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}(a_{1}{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})
, and let
L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma)
denote the space of functions
u\in L^{2}_{\textit{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
such that
\smash{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5muk_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}})}\,u\in L^{2}(\Sigma)}
. Such functions are
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}
-pseudo-periodic in the
(a_{1}{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})
–direction and decay in the transverse direction:

\forall\;u\in L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma),\quad u({\bm{x}}+a_{1}\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5muk_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}\,u({\bm{x}}),\quad\zeta\mapsto u({\bm{x}}+\zeta\,{\mathfrak{v}}_{2})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}),\quad{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

Then Theorem
7.1
reduces to:

Theorem A.2
.

Let
{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}={\mathbf{v}}_{1}+r{\mathbf{v}}_{2}
, and assume that
r=b_{1}/a_{1}
where
b_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
a_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
are coprime, with the convention that
(a_{1},b_{1})=(1,0)
if
r=0
. Let
{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}
and consider the operator
\mathcal{T}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\in\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma),L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
and its adjoint

\displaystyle\forall\;u\in L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma),\qquad\mathcal{T}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,u(\zeta)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{1}(\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}}}\,u)\mskip 1.5mu(\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu(a_{1}{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})+\zeta\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}\mskip 1.5mu)\,d\mathrm{y}_{1},

\displaystyle\forall\;\alpha\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{C}^{2}),\qquad\mathcal{T}^{*}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\alpha({\bm{x}})

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\alpha({\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{x}}).

From these operators, define
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\mu):=\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}
where
\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}
is identified with the multiplication operator. Moreover, let
T_{a}:\alpha\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})\mapsto\alpha(\cdot-a)
and
\eta>0
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
7.1
, there exist constants
\delta_{0},\mu_{0}>0
such that if

\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\quad\mu\in(-\mu_{0},\mu_{0}),\quad\min_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}}\Big[\operatorname{dist}\big(z,\;\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\mu)\big)\Big]>\eta,

then setting
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu
, the operator
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}-\delta\,z
is invertible on
L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
, and the following holds:

(a).

Zigzag-type edges: if
a_{1}\neq b_{1}\mod 3
, then

\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}}{\delta}-z\bigg)^{-1}=\int^{\oplus}_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{K}}}(\mu)^{*}\,T^{*}_{\delta\mskip 1.5mus}\,\big(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(\mu)-z\big)^{-1}\,T_{\delta\mskip 1.5mus}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{K}}}(\mu)\;ds+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})

(A.2)

(b).

Armchair-type edges: if
a_{1}=b_{1}\mod 3
, then

\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}}{\delta}-z\bigg)^{-1}\\
=\sum_{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}\in\{{\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}\}}\int^{\oplus}_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\mu)^{*}\,T^{*}_{\delta\mskip 1.5mus}\,\big(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\mu)-z\big)^{-1}\,T_{\delta\mskip 1.5mus}\,\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{K}}_{\star}}(\mu)\;ds+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4}),\

(A.3)

where the error terms in (
A.2
) and (
A.3
) are with respect to the
\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),H^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}},{\bm{x}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))
norm.

Proof
.

For simplicity, we present the calculations for zigzag-type edges and for
\mu=0
, the extension to armchair-type edges or to
\mu\neq 0
being straightforward. By Proposition
A.1
, for zigzag-type edges, one has
\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})=\{{\mathbf{K}}\}\times a_{1}\mathbb{Z}
and
\gamma_{\mathbf{I}}=0
for
{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})
and for
\delta
small enough. In particular, from (
7.1
),
\varphi_{({\mathbf{K}},m)}({\bm{x}},s):=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2}+2\pi m)\mskip 1.5mus}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}}({\bm{x}})
for
m\in a_{1}\mathbb{Z}
. Our goal is to show that in this case, the dominant term in (
7.13
) coincides with the dominant term in (
A.2
), that is,

\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}\}\times a_{1}\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}\;\big(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{D}^{\mathbf{K}}(0)-z\mskip 1.5mu\big)^{-1}\;\mathcal{J}_{\delta,{\mathbf{I}}}=\int^{\oplus}_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{K}}\,\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,T^{*}_{\delta\mskip 1.5mus}\,\big(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(0)-z\big)^{-1}\,T_{\delta\mskip 1.5mus}\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{K}}\;ds.

(A.4)

Fix
m\in a_{1}\mathbb{Z}
. By a density argument, since the operators in (
A.4
) are bounded, it is sufficient to show (
A.4
) for functions
F\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
such that
\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\,{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,F\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
. For any
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
, using the change of variables
{\bm{y}}\mapsto{\bm{y}}+\zeta{\mathbf{v}}_{2}
in the definition (
7.2
) of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}
leads to

\mathcal{T}_{({\mathbf{K}},m)}\,F(\zeta)=\int_{\Omega-\zeta{\mathbf{v}}_{2}}(\mskip 1.5mu\overline{\varphi_{({\mathbf{K}},m)}}\,F\mskip 1.5mu)\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{y}}+\zeta{\mathbf{v}}_{2},-{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{y}})\,d{\bm{y}}=\int_{\Omega}(\mskip 1.5mu\overline{\varphi_{({\mathbf{K}},m)}}\,F\mskip 1.5mu)\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{y}}+\zeta{\mathbf{v}}_{2},-{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{y}})\,d{\bm{y}},

where the last equality follows from the
\Lambda
–periodicity of the integrand with respect to
{\bm{y}}
. We apply the operator
\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(0)-z)^{-1}\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}
, interchange it with the integral over
\Omega
, and use the simplified expression for
\varphi_{({\mathbf{K}},m)}
to obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(0)

\displaystyle-z)^{-1}\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{({\mathbf{K}},m)}\,F(\zeta)=\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi m\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{y}}}\,g({\bm{y}},\zeta)\,d{\bm{y}},\quad\text{where}\quad

\displaystyle g({\bm{y}},\cdot)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{y}}}\;\big[\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(0)-z)^{-1}\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\big]\,f({\bm{y}},\cdot),\quad\text{with}\quad

\displaystyle f({\bm{y}},\zeta)

\displaystyle:=\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}}({\bm{y}}+\zeta{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\,F({\bm{y}}+\zeta{\mathbf{v}}_{2},-{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{y}}).

Applying
\mathcal{T}^{*}_{({\mathbf{K}},m)}
and using
\mathcal{J}_{\delta,({\mathbf{K}},m)}=\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{({\mathbf{K}},m)}
, we find that the left-hand side of (
A.4
) is given by

\!\!\!\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}\}\times a_{1}\mathbb{Z}}\!\!\!\!\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\,\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(0)-z)^{-1}\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{x}},s)=\sum_{m\in a_{1}\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{T}^{*}_{({\mathbf{K}},m)}\,\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(0)-z)^{-1}\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{({\mathbf{K}},m)}\,F\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{x}},s)\\
=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\mskip 1.5mus}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\sum_{m\in a_{1}\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi m\mskip 1.5mus}\,\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi m\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{y}}}\,g({\bm{y}},{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}))\,d{\bm{y}}.

(A.5)

To further reformulate (
A.5
), we will rely on the following Fourier series identity: since the vector
2\pi\mskip 1.5mua_{1}{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}=2\pi(-b_{1}{\mathbf{k}}_{1}+a_{1}{\mathbf{k}}_{2})
belongs to
\Lambda^{*}
and
|\Omega|=|{\mathbf{v}}_{1}\wedge{\mathbf{v}}_{2}|=1
, for
u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}/\Lambda)
,

\sum_{m\in a_{1}\,\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi m\mskip 1.5mus}\,\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi m\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot{\bm{y}}}\,u({\bm{y}})\,d{\bm{y}}=\int_{0}^{1}u(\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu(a_{1}{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})-s\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{2})\,d\mathrm{y}_{1},\quad s\in\mathbb{R}.

(A.6)

In fact, this identity is easily verified for
u({\bm{y}})=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{k}}\cdot{\bm{y}}}
,
{\mathbf{k}}\in\Lambda^{*}
; it extends to trigonometric polynomials by linearity, and then to elements of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}/\Lambda)
by density.

We now note that
\zeta\mapsto g({\bm{y}},\zeta)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
{\bm{y}}\mapsto g({\bm{y}},\zeta)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}/\Lambda)
, the latter following from the definition (
5.2
) of
L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})
and from the fact that
\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}}\in L^{2}_{{\mathbf{K}}}
. This allows us to apply the identity (
A.6
) to
u:{\bm{y}}\mapsto g({\bm{y}},{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2}))
so that (
A.5
) becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}\}\times a_{1}\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\,\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(0)-z)^{-1}\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\mskip 1.5mu({\bm{x}},s)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu({\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\mskip 1.5mus}\,\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\int_{0}^{1}g\mskip 1.5mu\big(\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu(a_{1}{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})-s\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{2},\,{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\big)\,d\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\Big]

\displaystyle=\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}\,\bigg\{\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(0)-z)^{-1}\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\int_{0}^{1}f\mskip 1.5mu(\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu(a_{1}{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})-s\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{2},\,\cdot\mskip 1.5mu)\,d\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\,\big({\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\cdot({\bm{x}}+s{\mathbf{v}}_{2})\big)\mskip 1.5mu\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{T}^{*}_{{\mathbf{K}}}\,T^{*}_{s}\,\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(0)-z)^{-1}\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\Big[\mskip 1.5mu\int_{0}^{1}f\mskip 1.5mu(\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu(a_{1}{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})-s\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{2},\,\cdot\mskip 1.5mu)\,d\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\Big]\mskip 1.5mu\bigg\}\,({\bm{x}}).

(A.7)

Finally, using the definition of
f
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}f\mskip 1.5mu(\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu(a_{1}{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})-s\mskip 1.5mu{\mathfrak{v}}_{2},\zeta)\,d\mathrm{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\overline{\Phi^{{\mathbf{K}}}(\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu(a_{1}{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})+(\zeta-s)\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{v}}_{2})}\,F(\mathrm{y}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu(a_{1}{\mathfrak{v}}_{1})+(\zeta-s)\mskip 1.5mu{\mathbf{v}}_{2},s)\,d\mathrm{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=\big[\mskip 1.5muT_{s}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{K}}F(\cdot\,,s)\mskip 1.5mu\big](\zeta).

(A.8)

By combining (
A.7
) and (
A.8
), we find that the left-hand side of (
A.4
) is given by

\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\{{\mathbf{K}}\}\times a_{1}\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{T}^{*}_{\mathbf{I}}\,\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(0)-z)^{-1}\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{I}}\,F\mskip 1.5mu(\cdot\,,s)=\mathcal{T}^{*}_{{\mathbf{K}}}\,\mathcal{U}^{*}_{\delta}\,T^{*}_{\delta\mskip 1.5mus}\,(\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}}(0)-z)^{-1}\,T_{\delta\mskip 1.5mus}\,\mathcal{U}_{\delta}\,\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{K}}F(\cdot\,,s),

where the right-hand side in the above equality is exactly the right-hand side in (
A.4
). Therefore, the proof of (
A.4
), or equivalently, Theorem
A.2
, is complete.

Theorem
A.2
can be established more directly by exploiting the fibered nature of
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}
, together with results on rational edges. In fact, for
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}\in[-\pi,\pi]
, consider the two-dimensional Schrödinger operator

\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(s):=-\nabla\cdot A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}({\bm{x}},s)\,\nabla+V({\bm{x}})\quad\textnormal{acting on $L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma)$}.

The operator
\smash{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(s)}
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
, is a dislocation of
\smash{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(0)=-\nabla\cdot A^{\delta}({\bm{x}})\,\nabla+V({\bm{x}})}
, whose eigenpairs define edge states (
3.3
); see also Remark
3.2.

(
a
)

. In particular, given the dependence of
A^{\delta}_{\textnormal{aug}}
in
s
, the results in
[
61
,
111
,
46
,
43
]
can be applied to
\smash{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(s)}
by simply replacing the domain wall
\kappa(\zeta)
with
\kappa(\zeta+\delta\mskip 1.5mus)
. Furthermore, by definition (
5.2
) of
\smash{L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})}
,

L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}})=\int^{\oplus}_{\mathbb{R}}L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma)\,ds\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}=\int^{\oplus}_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(s)\,ds,\quad\textnormal{that is,}\\[8.0pt]
\forall\;F\in L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}),\quad{\bm{x}}\mapsto F({\bm{x}},s)\in L^{2}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(\Sigma)\quad\text{and}\quad(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}\,F\mskip 1.5mu)\mskip 1.5mu(\cdot\,,s)=\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(s)\,F(\cdot\,,s).

In particular, applying the resolvent expansion from
[
43
]
to each
\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}(s)
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
, leads directly to Theorem
A.2
, with
[
43
]
even yielding a
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/3})
–error term instead of
\mathcal{O}(\delta^{1/4})
. We refer to Remark
7.3
for an explanation of this difference in error orders.

Appendix B
Application of Theorem
7.1
to the functional calculus

Theorem
7.1
allows us to expand functions of
\delta^{-1}\,(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D})
using the Helffer-Sjöstrand formula
[
87
, Proposition 7.2]
,
[
35
]
: for
w\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
, and for a self-adjoint operator
\mathcal{A}
on a Hilbert space
\mathscr{X}
,

w(\mathcal{A})=\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi}\,\int_{\mathbb{C}}\frac{\partial\widetilde{w}}{\partial\overline{z}}(z)\,(\mathcal{A}-z)^{-1}\,dz\wedge d\overline{z},

(B.1)

with
\partial_{\overline{z}}:=(\mskip 1.5mu\partial_{x}+\mathrm{i}\,\partial_{y}\mskip 1.5mu)/2
for
z=x+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5muy
, and where
\widetilde{w}
is an
almost analytic extension
of
w
, namely
\widetilde{w}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{C})
,
\widetilde{w}|_{\mathbb{R}}=w
, and for any
N\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C_{N}>0
such that

\Big|\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\partial\widetilde{w}}{\partial\overline{z}}(z)\mskip 1.5mu\Big|\leq C_{N}\,\|w^{(N+1)}\|_{\infty}\,|\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits z|^{N},\quad\forall\;z\in\mathbb{C}.

(B.2)

Using the Helffer-Sjöstrand formula, Theorem
7.1
leads to the following result.

Corollary B.1
.

Let
w\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
with
\operatorname{supp}w\subset(-\theta_{\textnormal{gap}},\theta_{\textnormal{gap}})
. For
\eta_{0},\mu_{0}>0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, one can find
\delta_{0}\equiv\delta_{0}(\eta,\mu_{0},N)>0
with the following estimate: For any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,
\mu\in[-\mu_{0},\mu_{0}]
and
k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}={\mathbf{K}}\cdot{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\delta\mu
,

\bigg\|\mskip 1.5muw\mskip 1.5mu\Big(\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}}{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\Big)-\sum_{{\mathbf{I}}\in\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\sum_{j=-N}^{N}w\mskip 1.5mu\big(z_{j}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})\big)\;\Psi^{\delta,(0)}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{I}},j}\hyperref@@ii[item:notation_otimes]{\otimes}\Psi^{\delta,(0)}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{I}},j}\mskip 1.5mu\bigg\|_{\mathscr{L}(L^{2}_{k_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\parallel$}}}(\Sigma_{\textnormal{aug}}))}\\
\leq C\,\eta^{N}\,\|w^{(N+1)}\|_{\infty}.

(B.3)

Here,
z_{j}(\widehat{\mu})
is an eigenvalue of
\mathcal{D}^{{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{I}}}(\widehat{\mu})
, and
\Psi^{\delta,(0)}_{{\textnormal{aug}},{\mathbf{I}},j}
is introduced in (
4.18
,
7.7
) as a
\mathcal{O}(\delta)
–approximate eigenfunction of
\smash{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}}
with the approximate eigenvalue
E_{D}+\delta\mskip 1.5muz_{j}(\mu+\delta^{-1}\gamma_{\mathbf{I}})
; see also Section
4.4
.

Proof
.

We begin with the Helffer-Sjöstrand formula (
B.1
) with
\mathcal{A}:=\delta^{-1}\,(\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D})
:

w\mskip 1.5mu\Big(\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}}{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\Big)=\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi}\,\int_{\mathbb{C}}\frac{\partial\widetilde{w}}{\partial\overline{z}}(z)\,\Big(\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}}{\delta}-z\mskip 1.5mu\Big)^{-1}\,dz\wedge d\overline{z}.

We would now like to apply Theorem
7.1
; however, because the resolvent expansion holds only when
z
is bounded away from the spectrum of
\mathcal{D}^{\delta}(\mu)
, which can be the full real axis, we use an approximation argument. More precisely, following closely
[
136
, Step
3
in the proof of Theorem 3.7]
, for a self-adjoint operator
\mathcal{A}
and for
\eta>0
, we define

w_{\eta}\mskip 1.5mu(\mathcal{A}):=\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu2\pi}\,\int_{\{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}\,/\,|\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits z^{\prime}|>\eta\}}\frac{\partial\widetilde{w}}{\partial\overline{z}}(z)\,(\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{A}-z\mskip 1.5mu)^{-1}\,dz\wedge d\overline{z}.

(We emphasize that
w_{\eta}(\mathcal{A})
is simply a notation, rather than the result of applying a function to
\mathcal{A}
.) Next, we write

w\mskip 1.5mu\Big(\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}}{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\Big)-\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;w(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu))\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}=\mathcal{N}_{1}+\mathcal{N}_{2}+\mathcal{N}_{3},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{1}

\displaystyle:=w\mskip 1.5mu\Big(\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}}{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\Big)-w_{\eta}\mskip 1.5mu\Big(\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}}{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\Big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{2}

\displaystyle:=w_{\eta}\mskip 1.5mu\Big(\mskip 1.5mu\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\delta}_{{\textnormal{aug}},k_{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\parallel$}}}{\mathrel{\mkern 1.0mu\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}}}}-E_{D}}{\delta}\mskip 1.5mu\Big)-\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;w_{\eta}(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu))\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{e}^{\mskip 1.5mu\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}\,\mathcal{J}^{*}_{\delta}\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;\big[\mskip 1.5muw_{\eta}(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu))-w(\mathcal{D}^{\mskip 1.5mu\delta}(\mu))\mskip 1.5mu\big]\;\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{L}(\delta^{3/4})}\;\mathcal{J}_{\delta}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu\delta\mu{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\cdot{\bm{x}}}.

We first bound
\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
\mathcal{N}_{3}
. In what follows,
z=x+iy
. Using the Helffer-Sjöstrand formula and the definition of
w_{\eta}(\mathcal{A})
, one obtains
